Let me think about how to prove strong normalization for STLC with CBV and if-then-else.

The standard approach: logical relation (hereditarily strongly normalizing terms + well-typed).

We need strong normalizing on closed terms. But the relation needs to be defined on all terms and respect opening with free variables. For locally nameless with free variable contexts, typical TLC chapter "StrongNorm" uses a "SN" predicate on terms, defines an unary type-indexed relation "R T t" where:
- R Bool t := SN t (or well-typed + SN?)
- R (T1 -> T2) t := SN t /\ forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (app t v)

Then prove fundamental theorem for any term with free variables: we need interpretation of contexts: a context Gamma is "good" (Gamma ~ Gamma') if for each x, T with Gamma x = Some T, R T (Gamma' x).

But the issue: R T t requires SN t. For terms with free variables under CBV, free variables are values but they're not closed. Hmm, SN must be defined for open terms too (step only applies to closed terms since ST_AppAbs etc. require locally closed side conditions). Actually step can relate open terms too? Let's see: ST_App1 requires locally_closed t2 and t1 --> t1'. So maybe an open term can step. For example `(fvar x) (true)` — hmm t1 = fvar x doesn't step. An open term like `(lambda:T, bvar 0) true` is not locally closed? tm_abs requires lc_at (S 0) t, and if body is bvar 0, lc_at 1 (bvar 0) holds. So `(lambda:T, bvar 0) true` is locally closed and steps to open t v = true. So steps on open terms can happen with free vars inside.

The standard approach in TLC (Pierce) for strong normalization: Define SN on terms (open terms allowed), and prove:

- sn_abs: if for all v, value v -> SN (open t v) ... hmm actually the tricky part is app abs.

Plan: Define a logical relation over types with terms, where the base case is `SN t` (strongly normalizing via the inductive definition above). We need:

1. SN is closed under reduction: if t --> t' and SN t' then SN t (immediate by SN_intro? Actually SN has constructor SN_intro taking all one-step reducts; from SN t' you get SN t by SN_intro if all reducts of t are SN. But t --> t' only gives one reduct. So define SN as the inductive least predicate: `Inductive strong_normalizing := SN_intro : (forall t', t --> t' -> SN t') -> SN t`. Then to prove SN t from t --> t' and SN t', you need to show all t'' with t --> t'' are SN. Not immediate unless determinism.)

Hmm, but for a deterministic relation (CBV STLC is deterministic), t --> t' implies t' is the only reduct. But proving determinism adds work. Alternatively use the "accessibility" style: SN t := Acc step t. Then step closure is easy: Acc intro requires all reducts accessible, and from step t t' and Acc t' you can prove Acc t only if all reducts go to accessible terms; since relation is deterministic, yes. But with the given `strongly_normalizing` inductive, we can show strong_normalizing t <-> Acc step t? Let's see: strong_normalizing is defined as the least fixed point: SN_intro takes (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> SN t. That's exactly the "well-founded/accessible" predicate but as an inductive family without the requirement of well-foundedness of the relation globally. Actually `Acc R t` has constructor `Acc_intro : (forall y, R t y -> Acc R y) -> Acc R t`. And `strongly_normalizing` is structurally the same definition, just not parameterized. So strong_normalizing t <-> Acc step t should be provable by induction (both directions). Note however `Acc` is in Prop and its induction principle is fine.

But actually, we don't need `Acc`. We can prove "SN is closed under expansion" by a small lemma? Given the SN_intro constructor takes all one-step reducts. For deterministic step: if t --> t' then any t'' with t --> t'' equals t'. That needs determinism of step. With CBV and if-statements, determinism should hold but requires inversion work on step whose premises include locally_closed facts. Actually determinism holds regardless of side conditions: step relation constructed with unique rule shapes? Potential overlap: ST_App1 vs ST_App2 could both apply if t1 is a value and t1 --> t1'? Values don't step (need to prove). ST_AppAbs vs ST_App1: t1 is abs, can abs step? Values don't step, so no. App2 requires value v1; AppAbs requires t1 = abs; abs is a value, so if AppAbs applies and App1 also applies, App1 requires abs --> t1' — values don't step. So determinism follows from values don't step. But we can avoid determinism by using Acc as intermediate.

Alternative cleaner approach: Define SN as `Acc step t` (i.e., well-foundedness at t), prove target `strongly_normalizing t` from `Acc step t`. But actually the target is the given inductive `strongly_normalizing`, so we need to convert. Maybe easier: define `sn t := Acc step t`, do logical relation, and at the end show `Acc step t -> strongly_normalizing t` by induction on Acc. For Acc_intro: given forall y, step t y -> Acc y. By IH each Acc y -> SN y, so all step reducts are SN, so SN_intro. Induction on Acc: the induction principle for Acc is:

```
Acc_intro : forall (A : Type) (R : A -> A -> Prop) (x : A),
  (forall y : A, R x y -> Acc R y) -> Acc R x

Acc_ind :
  forall (A : Type) (R : A -> A -> Prop) (P : A -> Prop),
  (forall x : A, (forall y : A, R x y -> Acc R y) -> (forall y : A, R x y -> P y) -> P x) ->
  forall a : A, Acc R a -> P a
```

Yes, Acc_ind gives exactly the recursion where we have the nested accessibilities. So:

```coq
Lemma acc_sn : forall t, Acc step t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t H. induction H as [t Hacc IH].
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
  apply IH. exact Hstep.  (* wait IH : forall y, step t y -> strongly_normalizing y? *)
```

Hmm, Acc_ind's IH is `forall y, R x y -> P y` where P y := strongly_normalizing y, plus we have Hacc : forall y, R x y -> Acc R y. So indeed apply IH to Hstep gives strongly_normalizing t'.

and this is a *sound over-approximation*: it's the least set containing values and closed under step expansion. OK so determinism it is. Let's check determinism is truly straightforward. Step rules:

1. ST_AppAbs: app (abs T t) v --> open t v, requires lc (abs T t), value v.
2. ST_App1: app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2, t1 --> t1', lc t2.
3. ST_App2: app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2', value v1, t2 --> t2'.
4. ST_IfTrue: if true t1 t2 --> t1, lc t1, lc t2.
5. ST_IfFalse: if false t1 t2 --> t2.
6. ST_If: if t1 t2 t3 --> if t1' t2 t3, t1 --> t1', lc t2, lc t3.

Determinism: Inversion pairs:
- AppAbs vs App1: t1 = abs T t is a value; App1 needs abs --> t1' contradict value_no_step.
- AppAbs vs App2: App2 needs v --> t2' where v = v (the argument) and value v, contradiction.
- App1 vs App2: App1 needs t1 --> t1'; App2 needs value t1; contradiction value_no_step.
- App1 vs App1: same t1 --> t1' and t1 --> t1''; by IH t1'=t1''.
- App2 vs App2: same.
- IfTrue vs If: t1 = true and true --> t1', contradiction.
- IfFalse vs If: same.
- If vs If: IH on t1.
- IfTrue vs IfFalse: true = false impossible.
- AppAbs vs AppAbs: same.
- IfTrue vs IfTrue: t1 = t1.

But is `value t -> ~ step t t'` easy? Inversion on value gives 3 cases; in each, inversion on step won't match because step conclusions are app or if. Hmm, step conclusion could be app... for value abs, conclusion must be app (abs...) but the step constructors' conclusions have specific shapes: `tm_app (tm_abs T t) v`, `tm_app t1 t2`, `tm_app v1 t2`, `tm_if ...`. To rule out `tm_abs T t = tm_app ...` it's a constructor disjointness, inversion should solve it. But there's a subtlety: constructors in Prop (value and step), inversion on an equality of constructors of tm is decidable structurally. `inversion Hstep` where Hstep : tm_abs T t --> t' should find no matching constructors and close the goal. I believe `inversion` handles it. Actually because the index tm_abs T t doesn't unify with tm_app ... or tm_if ..., inversion yields no cases. Good.

Wait, but there's a subtlety: step constructors ST_App1 etc. have premises like `t1 --> t1'` and we're doing inversion on a concrete term; it should fail to unify `tm_abs T t` with `tm_app t1 t2`. Since tm constructors are disjoint, inversion derives False. But I recall `inversion` on inductive propositions with non-unifiable indices can sometimes just do nothing. Let me recall: For `Inductive foo : nat -> Prop := f : foo 0.` then `inversion (f : foo 1)`? Hmm, can't even type it. Here the statement is `step (tm_abs T t) t'`, and step's constructors conclude step (tm_app ...) ... — the index is a function of parameters, and `inversion` should detect the impossibility via discriminate on the constructor argument. I think inversion does: it will try to unify the conclusion with each constructor and fail, closing the goal by "no such constructor" (it uses discriminate). I'm fairly confident.

Alternatively prove `value_no_step` by induction on step derivation generalizing the value hypothesis: `forall t t', step t t' -> value t -> False`, then in each constructor, inversion on value. E.g., ST_AppAbs has t = app (abs T t) v, value (app ...) impossible; inversion on value H gives cases v_abs with (abs T t = app ...)? no wait value (tm_app ...) has no constructor, so inversion on value H closes immediately. Indeed `value` constructors conclude value (tm_abs...), value true, value false; none unify with value (tm_app ...) or value (tm_if ...). So `intros t t' Hstep; induction Hstep; intros Hv; inversion Hv` should work: each case concludes a step term that is app or if, and inversion Hv closes.

Good. Then determinism:

```coq
Lemma step_det : forall t t1, step t t1 -> forall t2, step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.

Let's work out the locally nameless details of the commutation. Define:

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | ...
  end.
```

Claim: for any x, u, body, sigma with sigma x = u and x not free in range of sigma (i.e., for all y, x not free in sigma y), we have subst sigma (open body (fvar x)) = open (subst sigma body) u.

Let me just include lc in R at every type. Define:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ Acc step t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ Acc step t /\
      (forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u))
  end.
```

Note: for R T1 u, R includes lc u, so ST_App1 works.

Now key lemmas:

(L1) `R_acc : forall T t, R T t -> Acc step t` (and R_lc).
(L2) `R_step : forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t' -> R T t`.
  Proof by induction on T.
  - Bool: need lc t and Acc t. lc t from step_lc (step t t' gives lc t and lc t'). Acc t from Acc t' and step t t' with determinism: `Acc_intro (fun t'' H => eq_rect ... (step_det H Hstep))`. 
  - Arrow T1 T2: need lc t, Acc t, and forall u R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u). Given R T2 (app t' u) and step (app t u) (app t' u) via ST_App1 Hstep (R_lc u). Apply IH_T2.
(L3) `R_neutral`? For the context interpretation at free variables: R T (fvar x). Proof by induction on T:
  - Bool: lc fvar, Acc fvar (no step). Prove `Acc step (fvar x)` by Acc_intro; intro t' H; inversion H. Should work since step conclusions are app/if; fvar doesn't unify.
  - Arrow: lc fvar; Acc fvar; forall u R T1 u -> R T2 (app (fvar x) u). Need R T2 (app (fvar x) u). Hmm, this is a bigger term; induction on T2 doesn't directly give it (IH_T2 gives R T2 (fvar x), not app). We need a generalized neutral lemma: R T t for any t that is "stuck" (no step) and lc. Hmm, but for arrow types, R requires applications to be R too. If t is stuck and u ∈ R T1, app t u is stuck too (since t doesn't step and t isn't a value — need t not a value; fvar isn't a value; app t u isn't a value). Then app t u is stuck. So define:

```coq
Definition stuck (t : tm) : Prop := locally_closed t /\ (forall t', ~ step t t') /\ ~ value t.
```

Hmm, need to show R T t for stuck t by induction on T. Bool: lc + Acc (no steps). Arrow: lc + Acc + forall u R T1 u -> R T2 (app t u). Need app t u stuck: lc (from lc t, lc u); no step: any step of app t u is AppAbs (t would be abs→value contradiction), App1 (t steps), App2 (value t contradiction). Not a value: app isn't value. Then apply IH T2 to app t u. 

But wait, is R T2 (app t u) via IH T2 requiring stuck (app t u)? Yes, and IH T2 is proven for all terms, so fine. So:

```coq
Lemma R_stuck : forall T t, stuck t -> R T t.
```
by induction on T. Base: lc, Acc_intro (intros; exfalso; apply Hstuck2; auto). Arrow: lc, Acc, forall u Ru, apply IHT2, prove stuck (app t u): lc; no step (inversion on step; cases): 
- ST_AppAbs: t = tm_abs ...; but stuck gives ~value t — abs is a value. Contradiction.
- ST_App1: t --> t1' contradicts no-step.
- ST_App2: value t contradicts ~value.
Also ~value (app t u): inversion on value (no app constructor).

So R (fvar x) follows by R_stuck with stuck (fvar x): lc yes; no step inversion; ~value inversion.

Great.

(L4) The beta/application lemma for abstractions:
```coq
Lemma R_abs : forall T1 T2 body,
  (forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u)) ->  (* or all u *)

  Acc step (tm_abs T1 body) ->  (* actually lc + ?? Acc of abs: can abs step? abs is a value and values don't step, so Acc abs automatically! *)

Indeed, value abs implies Acc abs (no steps, and no red... Acc_intro with no reducts; or value_no_step). So Acc not needed as premise. Need lc abs. Need forall u, R T1 u -> R T2 (app (abs T1 body) u).

Wait R_step goes the wrong direction: from step (app abs u) (app abs u') and R T2 (app abs u') conclude R T2 (app abs u). Yes! That's exactly expansion. - If value u: as above.

Without classical logic, how to do the case split on ∃u', step u u'? We can't decide it. But we can reformulate lemmas to avoid it. Notice: in both cases (has reduct vs stuck), the resulting R property is proven. The existence case split is only needed to choose which argument to apply. But we can prove `R T (app t u)` constructively by proving each component separately:
- lc: easy.
- Acc (app t u): Acc_intro (forall r, step (app t u) r -> Acc r). Invert the step; each constructor gives the reduct's Acc directly from either IH or hypotheses. This is constructive and doesn't need the case split!
- application property: for arrow T = C→D, forall w R C w, R D (app (app t u) w). For this, we similarly build R D by components recursively... We can prove the whole thing by induction on T for the *application* property. Hmm, but the "stuck" case is where no steps exist; the components approach needs, for the arrow application property, to show R D (app (app t u) w). We can attempt to prove this by the same method at type D, but with the term app (app t u) w. Since the type decreases (D is a subterm of T), we can use induction on T! That's the key: define the property by induction on the type, not on the term.

Let me define a lemma:

```coq
Lemma R_app_closure : forall T1 T2 t u,
  R (T1 -> T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u).
```
This is just the arrow component — trivially true by definition. The hard lemma is:

```coq
Lemma R_abs_intro: forall T1 T2 body,
  lc (tm_abs T1 body) ->
  (forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u)) ->
  R (T1 -> T2) (tm_abs T1 body).
```
We need: forall u R T1 u, R T2 (app abs u). Let's prove this directly by induction on Acc u (which we have constructively from R T1 u). In the Acc_ind case, IH: forall u', step u u' -> R T2 (app abs u').

Goal R T2 (app abs u). We use a helper lemma proven by induction on T2:

```coq
Lemma R_abs_app : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc (tm_abs T1 body) ->
  (forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u)) ->
  forall u, Acc step u -> R T1 u ->
    (forall u', step u u' -> R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u')) ->
    R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u).
```

This is basically "if all reducts of u give R, then app abs u ∈ R T2", plus base case value u. Prove by induction on T2:
- T2 = Bool: components lc + Acc.
  lc: lc_app, lc_abs, R_lc u.
  Acc: Acc_intro. intros r Hr. inversion Hr:
    * ST_AppAbs: u value. Hmm, but in this lemma we might not be in the value case; we need Hbody to show R T2 (open body u) → Acc open body u. If u is a value, we have Hbody. So in the AppAbs case, use Hbody (value u) Ru → R_acc.
    * ST_App1: abs steps, contradiction.
    * ST_App2: u → u'; apply R_acc to Hred u' Hstep.
  Good, this handles both value and non-value uniformly! Because AppAbs case provides its own premise value u.
- T2 = C→D: components lc + Acc (same as above, reusing the inverted steps) + forall w R C w, R D (app (app abs u) w).
  Now goal R D (app (app abs u) w). Use IH on T2 = C→D? No wait, induction on T2 where T2 = C→D means IH applies to C and D. We want to apply the lemma at type D, with "term" (tm_app (tm_abs T1 body) u) and argument w:
  
  IH_D : forall T1 T2 body? no, the lemma is parameterized by T1, T2, body, and u. Hmm, our term app (app abs u) w: to apply the lemma, we'd need it in the form app (tm_abs T1' body') u'' — but the function part is app abs u, not an abstraction. So that's not the right generalization.

Let me instead generalize the lemma over an arbitrary function term f:

```coq
Lemma R_app_of_reds : forall T1 T2 f u,
  locally_closed f ->
  Acc step f? ...
```

Hmm. Let me think about the structure differently.

Standard approach (from Girard's reducibility candidates / TLC): Prove two key lemmas by induction on type:
1. `R T t -> sn t` (using their sn definition).
2. `R T t` closed under expansion: `sn t -> (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') -> R T t`. — This is exactly my `R_of_reds` but for SN t instead of Acc. Let me reconsider this lemma. Earlier I noted the arrow case problem: for t = abs, no reducts, so hypothesis vacuous, and we can't show forall u, R B (t u). Actually here's a cleaner known formulation (from the "Semantics" lecture notes by Charguéraud? or the POPLmark solution): Define R with the arrow case using values only, then the "expansion lemma" is:

`R T is closed under expansion` where expansion means: if t reduces in one step to t' ∈ R T, then t ∈ R T. For arrow type this requires showing for all values v ∈ R A, app t v ∈ R B. We have R_step for the reduction t v → t' v? But t v → t' v requires value? ST_App1 requires lc v, which R A v gives (if R carries lc). So app t v → app t' v via App1 (needs lc v). Then R B (app t v) from R B (app t' v) by expansion at B (IH on type, since B smaller). This works! The arrow R only quantifies over *values* v, so the application property needed is only for values, and expansion at B handles it. Great: with the value-restricted arrow relation, R_step works uniformly!

Let's re-examine: R (A→B) t := lc t ∧ Acc t ∧ (forall v, value v -> R A v -> R B (t v)).

- Acc t from determinism.

Great, this works because we only need applications at values.

Now the beta case for abstractions: R_abs needs forall v value with R A v: R B (abs v). Since v is a value, app abs v → open body v by ST_AppAbs, and R_step at B with R B (open body v). ✓ — no induction on Acc v needed at all! Because the arrow relation only quantifies over values.

Then the fundamental theorem's T_App case: R (A→B) t1 and R A t2 need R B (t1 t2), but the arrow relation only gives the application property for *values* v ∈ R A. t2 need not be a value! Hmm. So one of the two directions requires the hard work. In the "all arguments" version, T_App is easy but beta is hard. In the "values" version, beta is easy but T_App is hard.

Now, R_of_reds itself: let me re-verify its proof, since it's used at type T2 (arbitrary). Statement:

```coq
Lemma R_of_reds : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.
```

Proof by induction on T.
- T = Ty_Bool: split; [lc | apply Acc_intro; intros t' H; apply R_acc with (T:=Ty_Bool); auto].
- T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2: split; [lc | split].
  - Acc: Acc_intro; intros t' H; apply R_acc with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2); auto.
  - intros v Hv Rv. Need R T2 (tm_app t v). Apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t v):
    * lc: R_lc t (from? we have lc t as hypothesis Hlc) and R_lc v (from Rv).
    * ~value (tm_app t v): inversion on value.
    * forall r, step (tm_app t v) r -> R T2 r. Invert:
      + AppAbs: t = tm_abs T body, with lc (abs) premise; but ~value t and value (abs) (v_abs H) → contradiction. Need `v_abs` requires lc (tm_abs T body). We have it as a premise from ST_AppAbs inversion. ✓
      + App1: step t t' and r = tm_app t' v. Hypothesis Hred : forall t', step t t' -> R (T1→T2) t'. So Hred t' Hstep : R (T1→T2) t'. Its arrow component applied to v Hv Rv gives R T2 (t' v). ✓
      + App2: value t contradicts ~value t. ✓

Note: I need R_lc and R_acc as lemmas proven by induction on T. Also R (T1→T2) t gives lc t, Acc t, and arrow component. Fine.

So R_of_reds uses only the arrow component of R and R_lc, R_acc; no R_abs. Good, no circularity with R_abs (which is needed for the fundamental theorem's T_Abs). R_abs's proof uses R_step (expansion) and R_of_reds? Let me redo R_abs with the value-only relation:

```coq
Lemma R_abs : forall T1 T2 body,
  lc (tm_abs T1 body) ->   (* perhaps derivable from Hbody? *)
  (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open body v)) ->
  R (T1 -> T2) (tm_abs T1 body).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody. split; [exact Hlc | split].
  - (* Acc abs *) apply Acc_intro. intros t' Hstep. exfalso.
    eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | exact Hstep].
  - (* arrow component for values *) intros v Hv Rv.
    apply R_step with (t' := open body v).
    + apply ST_AppAbs; auto.
    + apply Hbody; auto.
Qed.
```

That's simple! Great.

Now the fundamental theorem setup with context interpretation and substitution.

Definitions:

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm := ...
Definition subst_lc sigma := forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).
Definition ctx_interp Gamma sigma := forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).
```

Fundamental theorem:
```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, subst_lc sigma -> ctx_interp Gamma sigma ->
  R T (subst sigma t).
```

Wait, but there's a subtlety: `subst_lc sigma` requires ALL sigma x lc, infinitely many. In the T_Abs case, we extend sigma with x ↦ v where v ∈ R T1 (lc v) — so subst_lc preserved. Good. But initially, for the empty context, we need a sigma with subst_lc; e.g., sigma := fun _ => tm_true (all lc) and ctx_interp empty sigma vacuous. Then the theorem gives R T (subst sigma t) for a closed t (subst does nothing if t has no fvars, but t might have fvars? typing in empty context for a term with free fvars is impossible, but we don't need to know that: the theorem with sigma=true gives R T (subst sigma t), and if t is closed, subst sigma t = t). Hmm, but is `subst sigma t = t` when t is locally closed? Locally closed terms can contain fvars! lc includes fvars. So a locally closed term with fvars, typed in empty context is impossible, but proving that might be needed... Actually the target theorem: forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> strongly_normalizing t. We apply the fundamental theorem with sigma := fun _ => tm_true: get R T (subst sigma t). We need strongly_normalizing t, not of subst sigma t. Since has_type empty t T doesn't directly say t has no fvars. But wait: can a term with a free variable be typed in the empty context? T_Var requires Gamma x = Some T, empty x = None. So no variable rule applies. But could fvar x hide in a subterm that never gets typed? Every subterm of a typed term is typed in the derivation (for abs, the body after opening is typed; but an fvar in the body stays an fvar after opening). So no. We can prove: `has_type Gamma t T -> forall x, In x (fv t)? -> Gamma x <> None`? Hmm. Alternatively, prove that subst sigma t = t when sigma is pointwise lc and... no, sigma maps fvars to arbitrary terms, so subst changes fvars.

Better: choose sigma = fun x => tm_fvar x (identity). Then subst sigma t = t for all t. subst_lc identity holds (fvar x lc). And ctx_interp: for x with Gamma x = Some T, need R T (fvar x) — the neutral/stuck lemma. For the empty context case, ctx_interp is vacuous. So the target theorem follows from fundamental with identity substitution:

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T, empty |-- t \in T -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)) by (apply fundamental; auto; intros; [apply lc_fvar | unfold ctx_interp; intros x T' H; rewrite H in *; discriminate?]).
```
Hmm, ctx_interp empty sigma: forall x T, empty x = Some T -> R T (fvar x). empty x = None; `None = Some T` is False. So intros; unfold empty in H; discriminate H.

Then subst id t = t: prove `subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t` by induction on t. Then rewrite, get R T t, then R_acc gives Acc step t, then acc_sn gives strongly_normalizing t.

But wait: for the fundamental theorem, the identity substitution requires R T (fvar x) for context variables — the stuck lemma. But also subst_lc identity (lc_fvar). Good.

Now the fundamental theorem's T_Abs case in detail. This is the crux with locally nameless + substitution.

Context: Gamma, T1, T2, t1, L. H : forall x, ~In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2.

```coq
Lemma subst_open_rec : forall sigma k u t,
  (forall x, locally_closed (sigma x)) ->

OK so we need FV and freshness. Let's define free_vars or "occurs" as a decidable predicate and a list. Standard: 

```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

```coq
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).
Lemma fresh_notin : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
```
where fresh is S of max so it's > all elements. Prove: In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L, by induction on L. Then S(max) > max ≥ x, so not equal.

Lemma R_if : forall T c t e,
  R Ty_Bool c -> R T t -> R T e -> R T (tm_if c t e).
Proof.
  intros T c t e Rc Rt Re.
  assert (Hacc : Acc step c) by apply (R_acc _ _ Rc).

  induction Hacc as [c Hred IH].  (* motive: forall t e, R T t -> R T e -> R T (if c t e) *)
  intros t e Rt Re.
  apply R_of_reds.

    + (* If *) apply IH; auto. The IH : forall c', step c c' -> forall t e, R T t -> R T e -> R T (if c' t e). Apply with Hstep, Rt, Re.

But actually, hold on: in the fvar case, we need `open_rec k (subst sigma u) (sigma x) = sigma x` where sigma x is lc_at 0. So the induction works with the level incrementing: for abs, IH at k := S k with lc_at (S k) body. ✓.

So the fvar case needs lc_at k (sigma x), weakening from 0. ✓.

   Lemma open_rec_lc_inv : forall k u t, lc_at k u -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at (S k) t.

8. `value_lc : value v -> locally_closed v`. Easy inversion.

Now the R definitions and lemmas:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ Acc step t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ Acc step t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Lemmas:
- R_lc, R_acc by induction on T.
- R_step (expansion): forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t' -> R T t. By induction on T.
- R_step' (reduction closure): forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t -> R T t'. By induction on T.
- R_stuck: forall T t, lc t -> (forall t', ~ step t t') -> ~ value t -> R T t. Hmm, maybe combine lc and no-step: stuck. Let me define as:

  Lemma R_stuck : forall T t, locally_closed t -> (forall t', ~ step t t') -> ~ value t -> R T t.

      * lc t, value lc v.

        - App1: t → t', contradicts no-step t.
        - App2: value t contradiction.
      * ~value (tm_app t v): inversion.
  Note: for `In`? no.
  
  Hmm wait, in the Bool case, `Acc_intro` requires proving Acc step t: `apply Acc_intro; intros t' Hstep; exfalso; apply Hnostep with t'; auto`.
  
- `R_fvar : forall T x, R T (tm_fvar x)` — via R_stuck: lc_fvar; no step (inversion); ~value (inversion).
- `R_true`, `R_false`: R Bool true/false.
- `R_app_R`: application lemma (the hard one).

```coq
Lemma R_app_R : forall T1 T2 t u,
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t ->
  R T1 u ->

  induction Hacc_u as [u Hred_u IH_u].
  ...

Lemma R_abs_intro : forall T1 T2 body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs T1 body) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open body v)) ->
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 body).

  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody. split; [exact Hlc|split].

  - intros v Hv Rv. apply R_step with (t' := open body v); [apply ST_AppAbs; auto | apply Hbody; auto].

```

Lemma R_if : forall T c t e,

Now, the fundamental theorem. Let me write it:

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    subst_lc sigma ->
    ctx_interp Gamma sigma ->
    R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hlookup
                | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
                | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                | Gamma
                | Gamma
                | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3];
  intros sigma Hsub Hctx.
```

Hmm, naming the induction principle's arguments can be fiddly. Let me instead use `induction Hty; intros sigma Hsub Hctx.` and let Coq name things, then refer via the goal. That's error-prone to write blind. Better to think carefully and maybe write incrementally, compiling as I go.

Let me now think about the T_Abs case in full detail because it's the hardest.

Goal: R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (subst sigma (tm_abs T1 t1)) which reduces to R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)) by simpl.

- lc (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1)): requires lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1).

Definition subst_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=

And ctx_interp:
```coq
Definition ctx_interp (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).
```

Final theorem:
```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  empty |-- t \in T ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  pose proof (fundamental empty t T Hty (fun x => tm_fvar x) 
                (fun x => lc_fvar 0 x) 
                (fun x T' Hlookup => match Hlookup with end))
    as HR.
```
Hmm, ctx_interp empty sigma: `forall x T, empty x = Some T -> R T (fvar x)`. Since empty x = None, `None = Some T` is absurd. Proof: `intros x T' H; unfold empty in H; discriminate H`. But we also need R T (fvar x) in the result... the function can produce False elimination:
```coq
intros x T' H. unfold empty in H. discriminate H.
```
After discriminate, the goal R T' (fvar x) — discriminate proves anything? `discriminate H` where H : None = Some T' solves the goal by contradiction. ✓.

subst_lc identity: `intros x; apply lc_fvar.` (lc_fvar : forall k x, lc_at k (tm_fvar x); locally_closed = lc_at 0). Use `exact (lc_fvar 0 x)`.

Then subst_id rewrites: `fold (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)`? The result is R T (subst id t). `rewrite subst_id in HR` — subst_id : forall t, subst id t = t. Then HR : R T t. Then `apply acc_sn, R_acc; exact HR` or:
```coq
apply acc_sn. apply (R_acc T t). exact HR.
```

acc_sn:
```coq
Lemma acc_sn : forall t, Acc step t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t H. induction H as [t Hred IH].
  apply SN_intro. intros t' Hs.
  (* Hred : forall y, step t y -> Acc step y; IH : forall y, step t y -> strongly_normalizing y *)
  apply IH; auto.
Qed.
```
Wait, Acc_ind's IH: The induction principle Acc_ind: (forall x, (forall y, R x y -> Acc R y) -> (forall y, R x y -> P y) -> P x) -> forall a, Acc R a -> P a. So yes, after induction H, we get Hred and IH as above. Then SN_intro requires forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'. Apply IH. ✓.

With R T t := lc t ∧ strongly_normalizing t ∧ ... where strongly_normalizing is the given inductive:

R_acc-like lemma: R T t -> strongly_normalizing t. Fine.

R_step' (reduction closure): R T t and step t t' -> R T t'. Components:
- lc t' from step_lc.
- SN t': from SN t and step t t'. Given SN_intro : (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> SN t. Hmm, SN t is an inductive; to get SN t' from SN t and step t t' — inversion on SN t gives the function forall t'', step t t'' -> SN t''; apply to t'. But wait, is SN t's constructor's premise exactly this? Yes: SN_intro : forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> SN t') -> SN t. So inversion on SN t (which is SN_intro t H) gives H : forall t', step t t' -> SN t'. Apply. ✓ No determinism needed!
- arrow component: forall v, R T1 v -> R T2 (t' v): from R T2 (t v) and step (t v) (t' v) via ST_App1 (lc v) and IH at T2 (reduction closure). ✓.
So R_step' works without determinism.

R_step (expansion): R T t' and step t t' -> R T t. Components:
- lc t from step_lc.
- SN t from SN t' and step t t': SN_intro requires ALL reducts of t to be SN. We have SN t' for one reduct. Need determinism. So determinism needed here regardless.
  With determinism: intro t''; intros Hstep''; rewrite (step_det Hstep'' Hstep); exact SN_t'.
  Hmm, SN_intro's premise is forall t', step t t' -> SN t'. So `apply SN_intro; intros t'' Hstep''; ...`.
- arrow: same as before with IH at T2.

So determinism is needed for expansion. Fine, we'll prove it.

```
strongly_normalizing_ind :
  forall P : tm -> Prop,
    (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t') -> (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
    forall t, strongly_normalizing t -> P t.

Same shape as Acc_ind. Good.

Actually, using the given strongly_normalizing has advantages: R_acc gives strongly_normalizing directly, and the final theorem is immediate. Also, "SN t → all reducts SN" is just inversion/pattern matching. And no need to import Wf.

Let me redefine:
```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Hmm, but wait: the target theorem's `strongly_normalizing t` for closed well-typed t; R T t includes SN t as a component for all types. Good.

(SN expansion) `sn_step_inv`? From step t t' and SN t' get SN t; needs determinism:
```coq
Lemma sn_step_expand : forall t t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t' -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep Hsn.
  apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hstep''.
  assert (t'' = t') by (eapply step_det; eauto). subst. exact Hsn.
Qed.
```

(R_step') reduction closure by induction on T:
```coq
Lemma R_step' : forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T; intros t t' Hs Rt; simpl in *.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + eapply step_lc; eauto.  (* gives lc t' *)
    + eapply sn_step; eauto.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split; [eapply step_lc; eauto|split].
    + eapply sn_step; eauto.
    + intros v Hv Rv. apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t v).
      * apply ST_App1; auto. apply R_lc with (T := T1); auto.
      * apply Happ; auto.
Qed.
```
Wait: in the arrow case, IHT2 is the IH for T2, but the induction principle for ty gives IHT1 and IHT2. `induction T` on ty with T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 gives IHT1 : forall t t', step t t' -> R T1 t -> R T1 t', IHT2 similarly. Good.

(R_of_reds) with strongly_normalizing:

Lemma R_of_reds : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->

      apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t v).
      * apply lc_app; [auto | apply R_lc with (T := T1); auto].

(R_app_R): as designed with SN_ind instead of Acc_ind. Let me write:

```coq
Lemma R_app_R : forall T1 T2 t u,

Lemma R_app_R : forall T1 T2 t u,

```coq
Lemma R_if : forall T c t e,
  R Ty_Bool c -> R T t -> R T e -> R T (tm_if c t e).
Proof.
  intros T c t e Rc Rt Re.
  destruct Rc as [Hlc_c Hsn_c].
  revert t e Rt Re.
  induction Hsn_c as [c Hred_c IH_c].
  (* IH_c : forall y, step c y -> forall t e, R T t -> R T e -> R T (if y t e) *)
  intros t e Rt Re.
  apply R_of_reds.
  - apply lc_if; [auto | apply R_lc with (T:=T); auto | apply R_lc with (T:=T); auto].
  - intros Hv; inversion Hv.
  - intros r Hr; inversion Hr; subst.
    + exact Rt.
    + exact Re.
    + apply IH_c; auto.
Qed.
```
The If case: inversion of step (tm_if c t e) r with ST_If gives c → c', t2 = t, t3 = e, r = tm_if c' t e; IH_c c' Hstep : forall t e, R T t -> R T e -> R T (if c' t e); apply with Rt Re. ✓.

### Substitution definitions and lemmas

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.
```

Free variables:
```coq
Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.
```

Fresh:
```coq
Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).

Lemma in_le_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [|a L IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct H as [<-|H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eauto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H. apply in_le_max in H. apply Nat.nle_succ_diag_l? 
  (* H : S max <= max, contradiction *)
  apply Nat.lt_irrefl with (fold_right Nat.max 0 L)? 
  pose proof (Nat.lt_succ_diag_r (fold_right Nat.max 0 L)). lia.
Qed.
```
Using `lia` requires `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` Is Lia installed with Rocq 9.1? Yes, Stdlib includes Lia. Let me use `lia` for arithmetic conveniences. Otherwise use Nat lemmas. I'll try Lia.

Wait, but is `Nat.max` in scope? From PeanoNat import includes Nat.max. fold_right from List. OK.

```coq
Lemma subst_lc_pres : forall sigma t k,
  subst_lc sigma -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma t. induction t in k |- *; simpl; intros Hsub H; auto.
  - (* bvar *) exact H.
  - (* fvar *) apply lc_at_mono with (k1 := 0); auto. apply Hsub.
  - (* app *) inversion H; subst; constructor; auto.
  - (* abs *) inversion H; subst; constructor; apply IHt with (k := S k); auto.
  - (* true/false *) constructor.
  - (* if *) inversion H; subst; constructor; auto.
Qed.
```

Lemma subst_ext : forall sigma1 sigma2 t,
  (forall x, In x (fv t) -> sigma1 x = sigma2 x) ->
  subst sigma1 t = subst sigma2 t.

  - (* abs *) simpl. f_equal. apply IHt. intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl; auto.
  - (* if *) simpl. f_equal.
    + apply IHt1; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; apply in_or_app; auto.
    + apply IHt2; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; apply in_or_app; auto.
    + apply IHt3; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; apply in_or_app; auto.
```
Here the fv of if is `fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3` which parses as (fv t1 ++ fv t2) ++ fv t3. `apply in_or_app; auto` should work for each.

Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.

  - ST_App1 t1' t1'' t2' Hstep Hlc2: unify tm_app (tm_abs T t) v = tm_app t1' t2' → t1' = tm_abs T t; Hstep : step (tm_abs T t) t1''; but value (tm_abs T t) by v_abs Hlc; contradict value_no_step. Goal open t v = tm_app t1'' v (the reduct). exfalso.

  H2 inversion:

  - ST_App2: requires value t1, but Hstep : step t1 t1' contradicts value_no_step.

### Type of R and its reduction behavior

Note: `R` is a Fixpoint on ty. Since ty is an inductive in Type, and R returns Prop, the Fixpoint is fine. We need `simpl` to unfold R; define R with a `Fixpoint` so it computes.

Let me write:

```coq
Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.
```

Careful: Coq's guard checker for Fixpoint on T with recursive calls R T1 and R T2 — T1 and T2 are subterms of Ty_Arrow T1 T2, fine.

### Potential issue: strong_normalizing's induction principle (SN_ind) generated automatically. Using `induction Hsn as [t Hred IH]` should work. But note: `strongly_normalizing` is defined with a single constructor SN_intro. The induction principle:

```
strongly_normalizing_ind :
  forall P : tm -> Prop,
   (forall t : tm,
       (forall t' : tm, step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t') ->
       (forall t' : tm, step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->
   forall s : tm, strongly_normalizing s -> P s
```

So `induction Hsn as [t Hred IH]` gives exactly that. ✓.

But wait: there's a subtlety — SN_ind is a *strong* induction? No! It's the standard induction principle for the inductive predicate, whose premise includes `forall t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'`. That's fine: the inductive is an accessibility predicate, so its induction principle is well-founded recursion. ✓.

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HR : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty); auto.
    - intros x; apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hlook; unfold empty in Hlook; discriminate Hlook. }
  rewrite subst_id in HR.
  apply (R_sn T t HR).
Qed.
```

  - (* fvar *) constructor.
  - (* app *) constructor; eauto.

Qed.

Let me write it as induction on t with `inversion H; subst` then cases:
- bvar: `constructor; lia`.
- fvar: constructor.
- app: `constructor; [apply IHt1 with (k1:=k1)| apply IHt2 with (k1:=k1)]; auto.` Actually IHt1 takes k1 k2 Hle. Since k1, k2 are universally quantified in IH? - if: constructor; eauto.

#### open_rec_lc

```coq
Lemma open_rec_lc : forall k u t, lc_at k t -> open_rec k u t = t.
Proof.
  intros k u t. revert k.
  induction t; intros k H; simpl in *.
  - (* bvar *) inversion H; subst. destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E; auto.
    apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. lia.
  - (* fvar *) reflexivity.
  - (* app *) inversion H; subst. rewrite IHt1; auto. rewrite IHt2; auto.
  - (* abs *) inversion H; subst. simpl. rewrite IHt with (k := S k); auto.
  - (* true *) reflexivity.
  - (* false *) reflexivity.
  - (* if *) inversion H; subst. rewrite IHt1; auto. rewrite IHt2; auto. rewrite IHt3; auto.
Qed.
```
Wait, the bvar case: H : lc_at k (tm_bvar n). inversion gives n < k. Goal `(if Nat.eqb k n then u else tm_bvar n) = tm_bvar n`. destruct eqb; auto; then E : (k =? n) = true → k = n → n < k contradiction. `apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; lia.` ✓.

Hmm, but the goal after destruct: in the true branch, goal is `u = tm_bvar n`? Wait no: if eqb k n is true, then open_rec returns u, so goal u = tm_bvar n?? Let me recompute: open_rec k u (tm_bvar n) = if Nat.eqb k n then u else tm_bvar n. We want this = tm_bvar n. If eqb true, then k = n, and lc_at k (bvar n) requires n < k = n, contradiction. So the true branch is contradictory. But after destruct, the goal would be `u = tm_bvar n` in the true branch — we can't prove it directly; we derive contradiction from E and the lc hypothesis. Wait, but `inversion H; subst` — hmm, H : lc_at k (tm_bvar n). Does inversion substitute n? lc_bvar : forall k i, i < k -> lc_at k (tm_bvar i). Unifying conclusion lc_at k (tm_bvar n) with lc_at k' (tm_bvar i') gives k'=k, i'=n, and premise n < k. After inversion, the hypothesis `H1 : n < k` (or similar). So in the true branch: E : (k =? n) = true → k = n; H1 : n < k; lia. But the goal in that branch is u = tm_bvar n; `exfalso; lia`? Actually if we can prove False from H1 and E, then `exfalso; lia` works, or `lia` directly proves any goal when hypotheses are contradictory! Lia proves goals over arithmetic; here the goal is an equality of terms, not arithmetic. But `exfalso; lia` works if the context is contradictory.

Let me write:
```coq
  - (* bvar *) inversion H; subst. destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E; auto.
    apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. exfalso. lia.
```
`auto` in the false branch: goal `tm_bvar n = tm_bvar n` ✓ (after simpl, if false then bvar n). Actually with destruct, the if reduces. Let me be careful: after `destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E`, Coq replaces the occurrence in the goal with true/false and simplifies? `destruct` on a boolean expression in the goal will reduce the if. I think the goal becomes `u = tm_bvar n` / `tm_bvar n = tm_bvar n`. `auto` solves the latter. For the former, prove False. ✓.

#### lc_at_open_rec

```coq
Lemma lc_at_open_rec : forall k u t,
  lc_at k u -> lc_at (S k) t -> lc_at k (open_rec k u t).

  - (* bvar *) destruct (Nat.eqb k n) eqn:E.
    + apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; exact Hu.

       (* H : lc_at (S k) (bvar n) gives n < S k; goal lc_at k (bvar n): n < k. But if k = n? handled by E false means k ≠ n. So n < S k and n ≠ k → n < k. *)

  - (* app *) inversion H; subst. constructor; auto.

  - true: constructor.
  - false: constructor.
  - if: inversion H; subst; constructor; auto.
Qed.
```

In the abs case: H : lc_at (S k) (tm_abs T t) → inversion gives lc_at (S (S k)) t. Goal: lc_at k (open_rec k u (tm_abs T t)) = lc_at k (tm_abs T (open_rec (S k) u t)) → need lc_at (S k) (open_rec (S k) u t); apply IHt with (k := S k): needs lc_at (S k) u (from Hu via mono) and lc_at (S (S k)) t ✓.

#### step_det

Let me just carefully write the case analysis. To make it manageable, I'll use the following proof structure:

```coq
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1; intros t2 H2; inversion H2; subst; auto;
    try (exfalso; eauto using value_no_step, v_abs).
Qed.
```

Will this work? Let's see each case after `inversion H2; subst`:

Case 1 (ST_AppAbs): H2 inversion produces:
 - subcase ST_AppAbs: subst; goal open t v = open t v; auto ✓.
 - subcase ST_App1: hypotheses Hstep : step (tm_abs T t) t1' (or similar) and Hlc; goal open t v = tm_app t1' v? Since auto fails, try does exfalso; eauto using value_no_step, v_abs. value (tm_abs T t) needs v_abs with lc premise (Hlc from the outer ST_AppAbs) — eauto using v_abs can construct value (tm_abs T t) from the lc hypothesis in context. Then value_no_step gives contradiction. ✓.
 - subcase ST_App2: Hstep : step v t2'; Hv : value v; contradiction via value_no_step. eauto finds it. ✓.

Case 2 (ST_App1): H2 inversion:
 - ST_AppAbs: value (tm_abs T body) via v_abs + Hlc premise; Hstep_outer : step (tm_abs T body) t1'; contradiction. ✓ eauto.
 - ST_App1: IH: forall t2, step t1 t2 -> t1' = t2. After inversion, Hstep2 : step t1 t1'', goal tm_app t1' t2 = tm_app t1'' t2? So in the outer case: term = tm_app t1 t2, reduct = tm_app t1' t2. H2 : step (tm_app t1 t2) t2_goal. Inversion:
   - App1 subcase: t2_goal = tm_app t1'' t2 with Hstep2 : step t1 t1''. Goal tm_app t1' t2 = tm_app t1'' t2. IH t1'' Hstep2 : t1' = t1''. auto should use IH? `auto` doesn't apply IH automatically unless it's in the context as a hypothesis. After induction, IH is a hypothesis (named IH H1 or similar). `auto` might use it? auto uses hypotheses of the form `forall x, ...` and applies them if it leads to an assumption? auto does simple apply of hypotheses? I think `auto` uses hypotheses via `assumption` and `apply` of local hypotheses? The default auto uses "simple apply" on hypotheses? Hmm, auto's proof search includes `assumption`, `reflexivity`, `trivial`, but also `apply` of hypotheses in context? I believe `auto` does use local hypotheses (it tries `apply H` for hypotheses H). Actually, the core auto tactic tries to solve goals by `assumption`, and for implications/foralls it introduces; it also uses "hints" from the hint database, but local hypotheses? I recall that `auto` does use hypotheses that are functions? Hmm, `eauto` does use local hypotheses via `eapply`? I'm not 100% sure. This suggests writing the proof with named hypotheses via `induction H1 as [...]` patterns. But step_ind's argument order: each constructor's premises. We can specify intro patterns:

ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, lc (abs T t) -> value v -> step (app (abs T t) v) (open t v).
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, step t1 t1' -> lc t2 -> step (app t1 t2) (app t1' t2).
ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> step t2 t2' -> step (app v1 t2) (app v1 t2').

### Another key check: R_lc and R_sn are Fixpoint lemmas by induction on T:
```coq
Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.

Actually simplest:
```coq
Lemma sn_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof. intros t t' H. induction H as [t0 Hred IH]. intros Hstep. apply Hred. exact Hstep. Qed.
```
Here induction on H with the goal `step t t' -> SN t'`; the motive abstracts t. Induction gives Hred : forall t', step t0 t' -> SN t' (note t0 is the abstracted variable), and the goal becomes step t0 t' -> SN t' after abstraction. Wait, when we `induction H`, the goal `step t t' -> SN t'` is generalized over t; the case goal is `step t0 t' -> SN t'` with Hred : forall t', step t0 t' -> SN t'. So `intros Hstep; apply Hred; exact Hstep`. ✓.

But careful: t' is fixed in the context; the induction motive is fun t0 => step t0 t' -> SN t'. Fine.

sn_step_expand:
```coq
Lemma sn_step_expand : forall t t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t' -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep Hsn.
  apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hstep''.
  rewrite (step_det t t'' t' Hstep'' Hstep). exact Hsn.
Qed.
```
step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2. So step_det t t'' t' Hstep'' Hstep : t'' = t'. rewrite → goal SN t' after rewriting t'' to t'. ✓.

Hmm, `rewrite (step_det ...)` where the equality is t'' = t' and the goal is SN t'': rewriting t'' to t' in the goal gives SN t'. ✓.

### R_step details

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t' -> R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hs Rt; simpl in *.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + exact (proj1 (step_lc t t' Hs)).
    + apply sn_step_expand with (t' := t'); auto.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split; [exact (proj1 (step_lc t t' Hs)) | split].
    + apply sn_step_expand with (t' := t'); auto.
    + intros v Hv Rv.
      apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t v) (t' := tm_app t' v).
      * apply ST_App1; [exact Hs | apply R_lc with (T := T1); auto].
      * apply Happ; auto.
Qed.
```

Careful: In the arrow case, `simpl in *` unfolds R in Rt, but also potentially unfolds R in the goal. After destruct, the goal is R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t which simpl unfolds to lc t /\ SN t /\ ...; but `split` should work on the unfolded form. Since we did `simpl in *` before destruct, the goal is simplified. OK.

But hmm, `simpl in *` could unfold R in weird places (like in hypotheses of the form R T1 v? no). Let's see: `simpl in *` simplifies all hypotheses and the goal. R T t' where T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 unfolds to the conjunction. Good.

One concern: in the arrow case, the `IHT2` applies to the type T2 with t := tm_app t v, t' := tm_app t' v. But we need R T2 (tm_app t v) from R T2 (tm_app t' v) and step. ✓.

Wait, actually the statement of R_step in the arrow case: we're proving R (T1→T2) t, whose third component is forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v). We have Happ : forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t' v) — wait, after destruct Rt where Rt : R (T1→T2) t', Happ is about t'. ✓. And we apply IHT2 with t := tm_app t v and t' := tm_app t' v, needing step (t v) (t' v) and R T2 (t' v). ✓.

### R_of_reds

Let me write with explicit bullets:

```coq
Lemma R_of_reds : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t Hlc Hnv Hreds; simpl.
  - split; [exact Hlc |].
    apply SN_intro. intros t' Hs.
    apply R_sn with (T := Ty_Bool). apply Hreds; exact Hs.
  - split; [exact Hlc | split].
    + apply SN_intro. intros t' Hs.
      apply R_sn with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2). apply Hreds; exact Hs.
    + intros v Hv Rv.
      apply IHT2 with (t := tm_app t v).
      * apply lc_app; [exact Hlc | apply R_lc with (T := T1); exact Rv].
      * intros Hval; inversion Hval.
      * intros r Hr. inversion Hr; subst.
        -- exfalso. apply Hnv. apply v_abs. 
           (* need the lc hypothesis from ST_AppAbs *)
           assumption.
        -- (* App1 *) destruct (Hreds t1' H) as [Hlc' [Hsn' Happ']]. 
           apply Happ'; auto.
        -- (* App2 *) exfalso; auto.
Qed.
```

The inversion cases: let me think about what hypotheses inversion produces with names. In the AppAbs case, Hr : step (tm_app t v) r. Inversion with constructor ST_AppAbs T0 body v0 Hlc0 Hv0. The unification: tm_app t v = tm_app (tm_abs T0 body) v0. Depending on which side Coq uses, it may substitute t := tm_abs T0 body (since t is a variable) and v := v0. Then Hlc0 : locally_closed (tm_abs T0 body), Hv0 : value v0, r := open body v0. Goal: R T2 (open body v0). We want exfalso; apply Hnv; apply v_abs; exact Hlc0. After subst, Hnv : ~ value (tm_abs T0 body), and Hlc0 gives value (tm_abs T0 body). ✓.

In the App1 case: ST_App1 t1 t1' t2 Hstep Hlc2 with tm_app t1 t2 = tm_app t v → t1 = t, t2 = v (probably substitution t1 := t or t := t1). Hstep : step ? ?; r = tm_app t1' v. Goal R T2 (tm_app t1' v). Hreds t1' Hstep : R (T1→T2) t1'. This term unfolds (after simpl in Hreds? we didn't simpl Hreds) to a conjunction. Since the goal R T2 (t1' v), we need the third component. Hreds t1' Hstep : R (T1→T2) t1' which is not syntactically a conjunction unless R is unfolded. Since R is a Fixpoint, `destruct (Hreds t1' Hstep) as [Hlc' [Hsn' Happ']]` should unfold R automatically (destruct on a reducible term? destruct requires an inductive type; R (T1→T2) t1' reduces to a conjunction, and destruct should see through the definition). I think `destruct` unfolds definitions during type checking. To be safe: `pose proof (Hreds t1' Hstep) as Hrt; simpl in Hrt; destruct Hrt as [Hlc' [Hsn' Happ']]; apply Happ'; auto.`

Hmm, `simpl in Hrt` where Hrt : R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1' — simpl unfolds R because T1, T2 are variables? R's Fixpoint matches on the type argument; with T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2 (a constructor), simpl should unfold. ✓.

In the App2 case: ST_App2 v1 t2 t2' Hv1 Hstep with tm_app v1 t2 = tm_app t v → v1 = t, t2 = v. Hv1 : value t, contradicting Hnv. Goal R T2 (tm_app t t2'). exfalso; auto. (Or exact (False_ind _ (Hnv Hv1)).)

Potential issue: after inversion, some variables get substituted in Hnv and Hreds; could make the terms weird but should be fine.

```coq
(** ** Helper definitions and lemmas (added for the proof). *)

(** Free variables of a term. *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).

Lemma in_le_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - contradiction.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H.
  apply in_le_max in H. lia.
Qed.
```

Wait: `destruct H as [-> | H]` where H : x = a \/ In x L. `[->|H]` substitutes x := a? The equation is x = a; `->` rewrites x to a? In destruct patterns, `[-> | H]` means the first case has equality; `->` substitutes. Then goal a <= max a (fold...); simpl in * turned fold_right Nat.max 0 (a :: L) into Nat.max a (fold_right ...). So goal a <= Nat.max a (...). `apply Nat.le_max_l` ✓.

Second case: H : In x L. Goal x <= Nat.max a (fold...). `eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; eassumption | apply Nat.le_max_r]`. ✓.

`fold_right` might be `List.fold_right` since we imported List. Using `fold_right` should resolve to List.fold_right (it's in the List module but exported). Actually in Coq, `fold_right` is defined in Datatypes/List? `List.fold_right` is the standard name, and importing List brings it. Fine.

```coq
Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)

  | tm_false => tm_false

      tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)

After the induction, the goal in each case is `forall sigma, subst_lc sigma -> ctx_interp Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)` with the appropriate Gamma/t/T substituted. Then `intros sigma Hsub Hctx` in each branch.

But wait: when we do `induction Hty as [...]`, the motive P will be `fun Gamma t T _ => forall sigma, subst_lc sigma -> ctx_interp Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`. However, does the `as` pattern work with the induction principle? Yes.

Hmm, one catch: after `intros Gamma t T Hty`, the goal is `forall sigma, subst_lc sigma -> ctx_interp Gamma sigma -> R T (subst sigma t)`. The induction will need to abstract Gamma t T in the goal; Hty : has_type Gamma t T is the induction hypothesis. ✓.

Let me now write the fundamental theorem branch by branch:

```coq
Theorem fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma,
    subst_lc sigma ->
    ctx_interp Gamma sigma ->
    R T (subst sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty.
  induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hlook
                  | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
                  | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                  | Gamma
                  | Gamma
                  | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3];
  intros sigma Hsub Hctx.
  - (* T_Var *) simpl. apply Hctx. exact Hlook.
  - (* T_Abs *) simpl.
    apply R_abs_intro.
    + (* local closure *) constructor.
      apply subst_lc_pres with (k := 1); [exact Hsub |].
      pose (x0 := fresh L).
      assert (Hx0 : ~ In x0 L) by apply fresh_not_in.
      pose proof (Hprem x0 Hx0) as Hty0.
      pose proof (has_type_lc _ _ _ Hty0) as Hlc0.
      unfold locally_closed in Hlc0.
      eapply open_rec_lc_inv; [apply lc_fvar | exact Hlc0].
    + (* body *) intros v Hv Rv.
      set (x := fresh (L ++ fv t1)).
      assert (Hnotin : ~ In x (L ++ fv t1)) by apply fresh_not_in.
      assert (HxL : ~ In x L)
        by (intro H; apply Hnotin; apply in_or_app; left; exact H).
      assert (Hxfv : ~ In x (fv t1))
        by (intro H; apply Hnotin; apply in_or_app; right; exact H).
      pose (sigma0 := fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then v else sigma y).
      assert (Hsub0 : subst_lc sigma0).
      { intros y. unfold sigma0. destruct (Nat.eqb x y); auto.
        apply R_lc with (T := T1). exact Rv. }
      assert (Hctx0 : ctx_interp (update Gamma x T1) sigma0).
      { intros y T' Hlook. unfold update in Hlook. unfold sigma0.
        destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E.
        - apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. subst y. rewrite E in Hlook. simpl in Hlook.
          injection Hlook as HT'. subst T'. exact Rv.
        - rewrite E in Hlook. simpl in Hlook. apply Hctx. exact Hlook. }
      pose proof (IHprem x HxL sigma0 Hsub0 Hctx0) as HRx.
      assert (Hcomm : subst sigma0 (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst sigma t1) v).
      { rewrite subst_open_rec with (k := 0) (u := tm_fvar x) (t := t1); [| exact Hsub0].
        simpl.
        rewrite subst_ext with (sigma1 := sigma0) (sigma2 := sigma) (t := t1).
        - unfold sigma0. rewrite Nat.eqb_refl. reflexivity.
        - intros y Hy. unfold sigma0. destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; auto.
          apply Nat.eqb_eq in E. exfalso. apply Hxfv. subst y. exact Hy. }
      rewrite <- Hcomm. exact HRx.
  - (* T_App *) simpl. apply R_app_R.
    + apply IH1; auto.
    + apply IH2; auto.
  - (* T_True *) simpl. apply R_true.
  - (* T_False *) simpl. apply R_false.
  - (* T_If *) simpl. apply R_if.
    + apply IH1; auto.
    + apply IH2; auto.
    + apply IH3; auto.
Qed.
```

Wait, in the T_Abs branch, the `Hprem` is `forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...`. And IHprem : forall x H, P ... where P Gamma t T := forall sigma, ... . So `IHprem x HxL sigma0 Hsub0 Hctx0` should typecheck. ✓.

Also, in the T_Abs branch's first subgoal (lc), `subst_lc_pres with (k := 1)` — subst_lc_pres : forall sigma t k, subst_lc sigma -> lc_at k t -> lc_at k (subst sigma t). Goal: lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). Apply with k := 1; goals: subst_lc sigma (Hsub) and lc_at 1 t1. ✓.

Now, `has_type_lc` on Hty0 : has_type (update Gamma x0 T1) (open t1 (fvar x0)) T2 gives locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x0)). Note `open t1 (tm_fvar x0)` is `open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x0) t1`. Then `eapply open_rec_lc_inv` with k := 0, u := tm_fvar x0, t := t1: goals lc_at 0 (tm_fvar x0) and lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x0) t1) = Hlc0. ✓.

But hold on: the T_Abs's R_abs_intro first premise is `locally_closed (tm_abs T1 (subst sigma t1))`. `constructor` gives lc_at 1 (subst sigma t1). ✓.

Now the T_App case: goal after simpl: R T2 (tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)). R_app_R : forall T1 T2 t u, R (T1→T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u). Apply should unify T1, T2 with the right types? The goal has R T2 (...) where T2 is the result type; R_app_R's T2 is also the result type. The first argument R (Ty_Arrow ?T1 ?T2) ?t; unify with IH1 : R (T1→T2) (subst sigma t1) — note IH1's type is `R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (subst sigma t1)` where T1 T2 are the theorem's variables. So apply R_app_R should infer. ✓.

T_If: R_if : forall T c t e, R Ty_Bool c -> R T t -> R T e -> R T (tm_if c t e). Goal R T (tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)). Apply. ✓.

Now has_type_lc:

```coq
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T Hty. induction Hty as [Gamma x T Hlook
                  | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IHprem
                  | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
                  | Gamma
                  | Gamma
                  | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
  - (* T_Var *) apply lc_fvar.
  - (* T_Abs *) apply lc_abs. 
    pose (x := fresh L).
    assert (Hx : ~ In x L) by apply fresh_not_in.
    pose proof (IHprem x Hx) as Hlc.
    unfold locally_closed in Hlc.
    eapply open_rec_lc_inv; [apply lc_fvar | exact Hlc].
  - (* T_App *) constructor; auto.
  - (* T_True *) apply lc_true.
  - (* T_False *) apply lc_false.
  - (* T_If *) constructor; auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, IHprem : forall x H, locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x)). ✓. Note `apply lc_abs` requires lc_at 1 t1 after unfolding locally_closed? Goal locally_closed (tm_abs T1 t1) = lc_at 0 (tm_abs T1 t1); `apply lc_abs` should work by unfolding the definition (constructor with the definition reducible). Actually `constructor` would be better: `constructor` applies lc_abs. ✓.

```coq
Lemma R_app_R_aux : forall T1 T2 u,
  strongly_normalizing u ->
  R T1 u ->
  forall t, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> R T2 (tm_app t u).

So induction abstracts u and the goal. Result: Hred_u : forall y, step u y -> SN y, IH_u : forall y, step u y -> (R T1 y -> forall t, R arrow t -> R T2 (t y)), goal `R T1 u -> forall t, R arrow t -> R T2 (t u)`. 

Then intros Ru t Rt.

```

  - exact Rt.

Another concern: the outer induction on Hsn_u and inner on Hsn_t: the order of reverts/intros. Let me write it as:

```coq
Lemma R_app_R_aux : forall T1 T2 u,
  strongly_normalizing u ->
  R T1 u ->
  forall t, R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> R T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 u Hsn_u.
  induction Hsn_u as [u Hred_u IH_u].
  intros Ru t Rt.
  assert (Hsn_t : strongly_normalizing t) by (apply R_sn with (T := Ty_Arrow T1 T2); exact Rt).
  revert Rt.
  induction Hsn_t as [t Hred_t IH_t].
  intros Rt.
  ...
```
Wait: after `intros T1 T2 u Hsn_u`, the goal is `R T1 u -> forall t, ...`. Then `induction Hsn_u` should abstract u in the goal. But the goal is a forall over u? No: Hsn_u : SN u is a hypothesis; the goal `R T1 u -> forall t, R arrow t -> R T2 (t u)` mentions u. induction on Hsn_u will generalize u in the goal → motive fun u => R T1 u -> forall t, ... . ✓. Then I get goal `R T1 u -> forall t, ...` with the new u. intros Ru t Rt. ✓.

Then `assert Hsn_t`, `revert Rt`, `induction Hsn_t`. But IH_u is in context and mentions... IH_u : forall y, step u y -> R T1 y -> forall t, R arrow t -> R T2 (t y). It mentions u? No, u appears only in the premise step u y. It doesn't depend on t. So induction on Hsn_t (which abstracts t) is fine as long as no hypothesis depends on t — Rt was reverted, IH_t not yet. ✓.

Then, in the App2 case, IH_u is available. ✓.

Then `R_app_R` follows:
```coq
Lemma R_app_R : forall T1 T2 t u,
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t -> R T1 u -> R T2 (tm_app t u).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t u Rt Ru.
  apply R_app_R_aux with (u := u); [apply R_sn with (T := T1); exact Ru | exact Ru | exact Rt].
Qed.
```
R_app_R_aux T1 T2 u Hsn Ru t Rt. ✓.

### R_if proof details

```coq
Lemma R_if : forall T c t e,
  R Ty_Bool c -> R T t -> R T e -> R T (tm_if c t e).
Proof.
  intros T c t e Rc Rt Re.
  destruct Rc as [Hlc_c Hsn_c].
  revert t e Rt Re.
  induction Hsn_c as [c Hred_c IH_c].
  intros t e Rt Re.
  apply R_of_reds with (t := tm_if c t e).
  - apply lc_if; [exact Hlc_c | apply R_lc with (T := T); exact Rt | apply R_lc with (T := T); exact Re].
  - intros Hv; inversion Hv.
  - intros r Hr; inversion Hr; subst.
    + exact Rt.
    + exact Re.
    + apply IH_c; [exact Hstep | exact Rt | exact Re].
Qed.
```
Hmm: `destruct Rc as [Hlc_c Hsn_c]` where Rc : R Ty_Bool c reduces to lc c /\ SN c. destruct should work. Then `revert t e Rt Re` — the goal is `R T (tm_if c t e)`; Hsn_c : SN c in context. `induction Hsn_c` abstracts c. Hlc_c : lc c depends on c! It would block/generalize. Hmm. Since Hlc_c depends on c, induction will need to include it in the motive or revert it. Let me revert Hlc_c too:

```coq
  destruct Rc as [Hlc_c Hsn_c].
  revert t e Rt Re.  
  induction Hsn_c as [c Hred_c IH_c].
```
Will Coq complain that Hlc_c depends on c? Yes, likely. Add `revert Hlc_c`? But then the motive must be fun c => lc c -> forall t e, ...; after induction we reintroduce Hlc_c. But in the If case, we don't need Hlc_c of the original c; we need lc of the reduct? No: R_of_reds's lc requirement uses Hlc_c of the current c (the induction variable). So revert Hlc_c and reintroduce after induction:

```coq
  destruct Rc as [Hlc_c Hsn_c].
  revert Hlc_c t e Rt Re.
  induction Hsn_c as [c Hred_c IH_c].
  intros Hlc_c t e Rt Re.
```
Hmm, after `revert Hlc_c t e Rt Re`, the goal is `locally_closed c -> forall t e, ...`. Reverting Hlc_c where Hlc_c : locally_closed c mentions c; this generalizes the goal to `locally_closed c -> forall ...`. Then induction on Hsn_c abstracts c throughout, yielding motive fun c => locally_closed c -> forall t e, ... . Then IHs: IH_c : forall y, step c y -> locally_closed y -> forall t e, ... . In the If case, we need R T (if c' t e) where c' is a reduct; with R_of_reds needing lc c' — get it from step_lc! Since step c c' gives lc c'. Hmm, but actually IH_c as stated needs lc c' as an argument. We have step_lc c c' Hstep : lc c /\ lc c'. So pass proj2. 

Alternatively, simpler: don't destruct Rc; keep Rc : R Ty_Bool c, and revert it. The induction motive fun c => R Ty_Bool c -> forall t e, ...; then in the If case, IH_c c' Hstep gives forall t e, ... with R Ty_Bool c' — but we need R Ty_Bool c'; do we have it? ### R_abs_intro uses R_step

```coq
Lemma R_abs_intro : forall T1 T2 body,
  locally_closed (tm_abs T1 body) ->
  (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (open body v)) ->
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) (tm_abs T1 body).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 body Hlc Hbody. simpl. split; [exact Hlc | split].
  - apply SN_intro. intros t' Hstep.
    exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | exact Hstep].
  - intros v Hv Rv.
    apply R_step with (t' := open body v).
    + apply ST_AppAbs; assumption.
    + apply Hbody; assumption.
Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl` on the goal R (T1→T2) (tm_abs ...) unfolds R to lc /\ SN /\ ... . Then split. ✓.
SN_intro's premise: forall t', step (tm_abs T1 body) t' -> SN t'. We prove by exfalso using value_no_step. ✓.

### R_fvar / R_stuck

Do we need R_fvar? For the final theorem with identity substitution, ctx_interp empty is vacuous, so R_fvar is not needed! Wait: `empty x = None` always, so ctx_interp empty sigma requires nothing. So we never need R for fvars in the final proof. But do we need it anywhere else? In the T_Abs case, sigma0 maps x to v ∈ R T1, not to fvar x. So no. Great, we can skip R_fvar/R_stuck entirely! 

Hold on — is that right? The fundamental theorem with identity substitution and empty context: ctx_interp empty id is vacuous. The induction in T_Abs creates sigma0 = update at x with v ∈ R T1. All context elements with non-None are handled by Rv or Hctx. So indeed we never need R T (fvar x). 

But wait, in the T_Abs case, when we extend sigma at x with v, the IH is applied to the opened body with sigma0. If the body's typing derivation contains T_Var for x, then Hctx0 handles it via Rv. ✓. No fvar relation needed.

So skip R_stuck/R_fvar. But hmm, R_of_reds's proof needed nothing about fvars. ✓.

Good, less work.

### Now let me double check R_of_reds's use in R_if and R_app_R with respect to `T` being arrow or bool: R_of_reds is proven by induction on T and works for both. ✓.

### Now let me think again about whether the whole approach is sound. The critical soundness check: R T t contains SN t. The fundamental theorem gives R T t for closed well-typed t. Final: SN t. ✓.

The relation is defined for all terms (with lc). The arrow component only quantifies values, and the application lemma bridges to arbitrary R arguments via SN induction + R_of_reds. I believe this is the standard CBV logical relation proof. Let me double check the R_app_R proof's AppAbs case: we use R_step' (reduction closure) to go from R T2 (t u) to R T2 (open body u). R_step' requires step (t u) (open body u) which is ST_AppAbs with value u — ✓ (u is a value in that branch).

But wait: in the AppAbs inversion case, the goal is R T2 (open body u) where the app term was tm_app t u with t = tm_abs T0 body. R_step' at T2: R T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T0 body) u) → step → R T2 (open body u). We get R T2 (tm_app ... u) from Rt's arrow component applied to value u and Ru. ✓.

```coq
(** ** Helpers for the strong normalization proof. *)

(** Free variables and freshness. *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).

Lemma in_le_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact H | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H.
  pose proof (in_le_max L _ H). lia.
Qed.
```

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof. *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).

Lemma in_le_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact H | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H.
  pose proof (in_le_max L _ H). lia.
Qed.

(** Simultaneous substitution for free variables. *)

Fixpoint subst (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst sigma t1) (subst sigma t2) (subst sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k1 k2 t, k1 <= k2 -> lc_at k1 t -> lc_at k2 t.
Proof.
  intros k1 k2 t. revert k1 k2.
  induction t; intros k1 k2 Hle H; inversion H; subst.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; eauto.
  - constructor. eapply IHt; [lia | eassumption].
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
  - constructor; eauto.
Qed.
```

Now step_lc in detail:
```coq
Lemma step_lc : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t /\ locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *) split.
    + constructor; [assumption | apply value_lc; assumption].
    + apply lc_at_open_rec with (k := 0).
      * apply value_lc; assumption.
      * unfold locally_closed in H. inversion H; assumption.
  - (* ST_App1 *) destruct IH as [IH1 IH1']. split; constructor; auto.
  - (* ST_App2 *) destruct IH as [IH2 IH2']. split; constructor; auto using value_lc.
  - (* ST_IfTrue *) split; [constructor; auto using lc_true | assumption].
  - (* ST_IfFalse *) split; [constructor; auto using lc_true | assumption].
  - (* ST_If *) destruct IH as [IH1 IH1']. split; constructor; auto.
Qed.
```
Hmm, in ST_AppAbs, the hypothesis is named H : locally_closed (tm_abs T t), and `unfold locally_closed in H; inversion H` gives lc_at 1 t. But careful: `assumption` in the first bullet needs lc (tm_abs T t) — H after unfold is lc_at 0 (tm_abs T t) which is definitionally locally_closed; assumption should still work (definitional unfolding). ✓.

In ST_IfTrue: goal locally_closed (tm_if tm_true b1 b2) /\ locally_closed b1. First: constructor (lc_if) needs lc tm_true, lc b1, lc b2; auto using lc_true — needs lc b1, lc b2 from hypotheses Hlc1, Hlc2. Hlc1 : locally_closed b1. `auto using lc_true` finds them by assumption. ✓. Second: assumption Hlc1. ✓.

Wait, the constructor premise order: lc_if : forall k t1 t2 t3, lc_at k t1 -> lc_at k t2 -> lc_at k t3 -> lc_at k (tm_if t1 t2 t3). Goal lc_at 0 (tm_if tm_true b1 b2); constructor gives lc true, lc b1, lc b2. auto using lc_true: lc_true : forall k, lc_at k tm_true. Hmm, lc_true has a k argument; auto using lc_true should instantiate. ✓.

In ST_App1: goal lc (app t1 b) /\ lc (app t1' b); IH : lc t1 /\ lc t1' (the IH from induction on step for the premise); Hlcb : lc b. destruct IH. split; constructor; auto. ✓.

ST_App2: IH : lc arg /\ lc arg'; Hv : value v1. split; constructor; auto using value_lc. For the first: needs lc v1 and lc arg; auto using value_lc: value_lc Hv : lc v1; IH gives lc arg. auto should use both. ✓.

Now the induction naming: `induction H` on step. The IH names: for ST_AppAbs no recursive premise (so no IH); ST_App1 has one IH (the step premise) named IH or similar. To be safe, use `induction H as [...]`? Or refer to `IH` by the generated names. When there's a single IH, Coq usually names it `IHH` or `IH`. Hmm, unpredictable. I'll compile and adjust; error messages will show the names? Not always. Alternative: avoid naming by using `destruct IH` — but we need the name.

Robust alternative: prove step_lc by induction on the derivation with an intro pattern that names the IHs:

```coq
induction H as [T body v Hlc Hv
              | a a' b Hstep IH Hlcb
              | w c c' Hw Hstep IH
              | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
              | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
              | a a' b1 b2 Hstep IH Hlc1 Hlc2].
```
This is explicit and robust. Same for value_no_step (induction on value) and others.

For step_det, I'll similarly use the explicit pattern.

Let me write step_lc with the explicit pattern:

```coq
Lemma step_lc : forall t t', step t t' -> locally_closed t /\ locally_closed t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H.
  induction H as [T body v Hlc Hv
                | a a' b Hstep IH Hlcb
                | w c c' Hw Hstep IH
                | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                | a a' b1 b2 Hstep IH Hlc1 Hlc2].
  - split.
    + constructor; [exact Hlc | apply value_lc; exact Hv].
    + apply lc_at_open_rec with (k := 0).
      * apply value_lc; exact Hv.
      * unfold locally_closed in Hlc. inversion Hlc. assumption.
  - destruct IH as [IHa IHa']. split; constructor; auto.
  - destruct IH as [IHc IHc']. split; constructor; auto using value_lc.
  - split; [constructor; auto using lc_true | exact Hlc1].
  - split; [constructor; auto using lc_true | exact Hlc1].
  - destruct IH as [IHa IHa']. split; constructor; auto.
Qed.
```

Hmm, in ST_IfTrue the args are t1 t2, premises lc t1, lc t2. Pattern `| b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2`. ✓.

```coq
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1 as [T body v Hlc Hv
                 | a a' b Hstep IH Hlcb
                 | w c c' Hw Hstep IH
                 | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                 | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                 | a a' b1 b2 Hstep IH Hlc1 Hlc2];
  intros t2 H2; inversion H2; subst.
  - (* AppAbs / AppAbs *) reflexivity.
  - (* AppAbs / App1 *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | eassumption].
  - (* AppAbs / App2 *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hv | eassumption].
  - (* App1 / AppAbs *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step.
    + eapply v_abs. eassumption.
    + eassumption.
  - (* App1 / App1 *) f_equal. apply IH. eassumption.
  - (* App1 / App2 *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [eassumption | eassumption].
  - (* App2 / AppAbs *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [eassumption | eassumption].
  - (* App2 / App1 *) exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hw | eassumption].
  - (* App2 / App2 *) f_equal. apply IH. eassumption.
  - (* IfTrue / IfTrue *) reflexivity.
  - (* IfTrue / IfFalse *) discriminate H? 
  ...
```

### The final theorem

```coq
Theorem normalization : forall t T,
  <{ empty |-- t \in T }> ->
  strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T Hty.
  assert (HR : R T (subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t)).
  { apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty).
    - exact Hty.
    - intros x. apply lc_fvar.
    - intros x T' Hlook. unfold empty in Hlook. discriminate Hlook. }
  rewrite subst_id in HR.
  apply R_sn with (T := T). exact HR.
Qed.
```

Note the theorem statement uses `<{ empty |-- t \in T }>` which unfolds to has_type empty t T. In the proof, `apply fundamental with (Gamma := empty)` — the goal R T (subst id t); fundamental's conclusion R ?T (subst ?sigma ?t) unifies with T, sigma := id, t := t. Its first premise has_type empty t T ✓ Hty.

`discriminate Hlook` where Hlook : None = Some T'. Since empty is a Definition (fun _ => None), after unfold it's None = Some T'. discriminate closes the goal R T' (fvar x)... Does `discriminate` prove arbitrary goals? `discriminate H` is like `exfalso; discriminate` — it should solve any goal when the hypothesis is a contradictory equality. In Coq, `discriminate` uses the hypothesis to derive False and concludes the goal. ✓.

But careful: the tactic pattern is on the goal `R T' (tm_fvar x)` after `intros x T' Hlook`. `unfold empty in Hlook` — empty is defined as `fun _ => None`; unfold should beta-reduce. Then `discriminate Hlook`. ✓.

Also `rewrite subst_id in HR`: subst_id : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t. HR : R T (subst id t). Is the id function syntactically the same as in subst_id? subst_id is stated as `subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t`; the goal has `subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t`. Alpha-equal. ✓.
