Lemma R_step' : forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t -> R T t'.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hs Rt; simpl in *.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc Hsn]. split.
    + exact (proj2 (step_lc t t' Hs)).
    + apply sn_step with (t := t); assumption.
  - destruct Rt as [Hlc [Hsn Happ]]. split.
    + exact (proj2 (step_lc t t' Hs)).
    + split.
      * apply sn_step with (t := t); assumption.

Qed.

```coq
(** ** Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof. *)

(** Free variables and freshness. *)

Fixpoint fv (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fv t1 ++ fv t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fv t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fv t1 ++ fv t2 ++ fv t3
  end.

Definition fresh (L : list atom) : atom := S (fold_right Nat.max 0 L).

Lemma in_le_max : forall L x, In x L -> x <= fold_right Nat.max 0 L.
Proof.
  induction L as [| a L IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + eapply Nat.le_trans; [apply IH; exact H | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_not_in : forall L, ~ In (fresh L) L.
Proof.
  intros L H. unfold fresh in H.
  pose proof (in_le_max L _ H). lia.
Qed.

(** Simultaneous substitution of free variables. *)

Fixpoint subst_tm (sigma : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => sigma x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_tm sigma t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 =>
      tm_if (subst_tm sigma t1) (subst_tm sigma t2) (subst_tm sigma t3)
  end.

Definition subst_tm_lc (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x, locally_closed (sigma x).

Lemma lc_at_mono : forall k1 k2 t, k1 <= k2 -> lc_at k1 t -> lc_at k2 t.
Proof.
  intros k1 k2 t. revert k1 k2.
  induction t; intros k1 k2 Hle H; inversion H; subst.
  - constructor. lia.
  - constructor.
  - constructor.
    + apply IHt1 with (k1 := k1); assumption.
    + apply IHt2 with (k1 := k1); assumption.
  - constructor. apply IHt with (k1 := S k1); [lia | assumption].
  - constructor.

```coq
Lemma step_det : forall t t1 t2, step t t1 -> step t t2 -> t1 = t2.
Proof.
  intros t t1 t2 H1. revert t2.
  induction H1 as [T body v Hlc Hv
                 | a a' b Hstep IH Hlcb
                 | w c c' Hw Hstep IH
                 | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                 | b1 b2 Hlc1 Hlc2
                 | a a' b1 b2 Hstep IH Hlc1 Hlc2];
  intros t2 H2.
  - (* ST_AppAbs *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + reflexivity.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | eassumption].
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hv | eassumption].
  - (* ST_App1 *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [eapply v_abs; eassumption | eassumption].
    + f_equal. apply IH. eassumption.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [eassumption | eassumption].
  - (* ST_App2 *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [eassumption | eassumption].
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [exact Hw | eassumption].
    + f_equal. apply IH. eassumption.
  - (* ST_IfTrue *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + reflexivity.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_true | eassumption].
  - (* ST_IfFalse *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + reflexivity.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_false | eassumption].
  - (* ST_If *)
    inversion H2; subst.
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_true | eassumption].
    + exfalso. eapply value_no_step; [apply v_false | eassumption].
    + f_equal. apply IH. eassumption.
Qed.
```

Concerns:
- In ST_AppAbs case, `inversion H2; subst` yields 3 subgoals? Possibly the App1/App2 cases might get automatically closed if inversion can't sensibly unify? No, they unify fine. Also, in the App1 subcase, maybe inversion names things differently, but `eassumption` finds the step hypothesis.
- `eapply value_no_step; [apply v_abs; exact Hlc | eassumption]`: value_no_step v t' : value v -> ~(v --> t'). eapply with the goal `False`? We're in `exfalso` so goal is False. eapply value_no_step: to prove False, it needs an instance of ~(v --> t') = (v --> t') -> False. It will unify with goals value ?v and step ?v ?t'. The first bullet proves value (tm_abs T body) via v_abs Hlc; the second finds the step hypothesis by eassumption. ✓.
- In the ST_App1 case, after inversion, subgoal order: inversion of H2 with H1 = ST_App1's conclusion step (tm_app a b) (tm_app a' b). H2's constructors: ST_AppAbs, ST_App1, ST_App2. Which order do the subgoals appear? Inversion generates subgoals in the order of the constructors of step: ST_AppAbs first, then ST_App1, then ST_App2. So bullets as written: AppAbs, App1, App2. ✓.
- The If cases: inversion of step (tm_if tm_true b1 b2) t2. Then my bullet structure breaks. Hmm. Inversion should detect the impossible case via injection/discrimination on the index and close it. I believe `inversion` closes impossible cases automatically.

- In the ST_AppAbs / App1 subcase: the step hypothesis might be `H : step (tm_abs T body) t1'`? Actually, after inversion and substitution, H2's premise is `step (tm_abs T body) ?a'`. eassumption should find it.

- For App2/AppAbs subcase: H1 = ST_App2 w c c' Hw Hstep (w is the value function). H2 = ST_AppAbs with w = tm_abs T body. Then the step premise of AppAbs is `step c t2'`? Now, the R lemmas. Let me write them.

```coq
(** The logical relation. *)

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall v, value v -> R T1 v -> R T2 (tm_app t v))
  end.

Definition ctx_interp (Gamma : context) (sigma : atom -> tm) : Prop :=
  forall x T, Gamma x = Some T -> R T (sigma x).

Lemma R_lc : forall T t, R T t -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros T. induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t H; simpl in H; tauto.
Qed.

Lemma sn_step : forall t t', strongly_normalizing t -> step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'.
Proof.
  intros t t' H. induction H as [t0 Hred IH]. intros Hstep.
  apply Hred. exact Hstep.
Qed.
```
Hmm, careful: `intros t t' H. induction H as [t0 Hred IH].` — H : SN t. The goal is `step t t' -> SN t'`. Induction on H generalizes t in the goal → motive fun t0 => step t0 t' -> SN t'. Then Hred : forall t', step t0 t' -> SN t' (with its own t' name), IH : forall t', step t0 t' -> (step t' t'? no...). Wait! The induction principle of SN: P t0 := step t0 t' -> SN t'. The IH is forall y, step t0 y -> P y = forall y, step t0 y -> step y t' -> SN t'. Hmm, the motive includes the goal's t'? Let me redo.

Goal before induction: `step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t'` where t' is a fixed local variable. SN_ind with motive P := fun t0 => step t0 t' -> SN t'. The constructor case:
Hred : forall y, step t0 y -> strongly_normalizing y
IH : forall y, step t0 y -> P y = forall y, step t0 y -> step y t' -> SN t'
Goal: P t0 = step t0 t' -> SN t'.

So `intros Hstep` gives Hstep : step t0 t'. Then `apply Hred; exact Hstep` gives SN t'? Hred t' : step t0 t' -> SN t'. ✓. Hmm, but Hred's quantified variable is named t' in the constructor? `forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'`. Applying Hred to Hstep : step t0 t' gives SN t'. ✓.

Wait, but the IH : forall y, step t0 y -> step y t' -> SN t' — that's a stronger hypothesis but unused. Fine.

Actually, careful with naming: `induction H as [t0 Hred IH]` where H : SN t. The `as` pattern for SN_ind's T_Abs-like case gives three names: the term, the SN-premise, and the P-premise. But is the induction applied with the goal still containing t'? Yes, and the motive abstracts only t (the index), leaving t' fixed. ✓.

Hmm, but there's a subtlety: after `intros t t' H`, the goal is `step t t' -> SN t'`. `induction H` generalizes t in the goal (and H). ✓.

```coq
Lemma sn_step_expand : forall t t', step t t' -> strongly_normalizing t' -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t t' Hstep Hsn.
  apply SN_intro. intros t'' Hstep''.
  rewrite (step_det t t'' t' Hstep'' Hstep). exact Hsn.
Qed.
```

`step_det t t'' t' Hstep'' Hstep : t'' = t'`. `rewrite ...` in goal SN t''. ✓.

```coq
Lemma R_step : forall T t t', step t t' -> R T t' -> R T t.
Proof.
  induction T as [| T1 IHT1 T2 IHT2]; intros t t' Hs Rt; simpl in *.

    + exact (proj1 (step_lc t t' Hs)).

        -- apply Happ; assumption.
Qed.

Here IHT2 : forall t t', step t t' -> R T2 t' -> R T2 t. eapply with goal R T2 (tm_app t v): unify with conclusion R T2 ?t → ?t := tm_app t v; goals step (tm_app t v) ?t' and R T2 ?t'. First bullet gives step (tm_app t v) (tm_app t' v) → ?t' := tm_app t' v. Second: R T2 (tm_app t' v) from Happ. ✓.

```coq
Lemma R_of_reds : forall T t,
  locally_closed t ->
  ~ value t ->
  (forall t', step t t' -> R T t') ->
  R T t.

There's a known proof by Tait for CBV where the logical relation on *values* is defined, and terms are related if they reduce to related values. Specifically:

Define T_T (sets of terms) and S_T (sets of values) mutually:
- S_Bool = {true, false}
- S_{T1->T2} = { λx:T1. t | ∀ v ∈ S_T1, t[x:=v] ∈ T_T2 }
- T_T = { t | SN(t) ∧ ∀ t' : t -->* t' ∧ value t' ⇒ t' ∈ S_T }
where SN(t) is all reduction sequences finite.

This is well-founded. Then:
- Values in S ⟹ in T? Need: v ∈ S_T → v ∈ T_T, i.e., SN(v) and all value reducts are in S. Values don't reduce (in STLC CBV, values are irreducible), so SN and only reduct v itself → need v ∈ S. ✓.
- T is closed under reduction: t ∈ T, t --> t' → t' ∈ T. Because SN descends and any t' -->* v is also t -->* v. ✓.
- Key application lemma: if t1 ∈ T_{T1->T2}, t2 ∈ T_{T1}, then t1 t2 ∈ T_{T2}. Proof: SN: t1 ∈ T → SN t1, t2 SN; need SN(t1 t2): by induction on SN t1 and SN t2 simultaneously? Definitions:

Fixpoint S (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => exists t, v = tm_abs T1 t /\ forall u, S T1 u -> T T2 (open t u)
  end
with T (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\ forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' -> S T t'.

Let me recall: For mutual fixpoints, Coq checks each recursive call (including calls to the other function) satisfies the guard condition: there is a "recursive argument" position such that the call's argument is a strict subterm of the current function's argument, and moreover after calling the other function, that function's recursive calls... Let me look at the standard proof more carefully. In TAPL's normalization chapter (or "hereditary normalization"), the key lemmas:

Lemma: If t ∈ T_T then t is SN and every value reduct is in S_T. (def)
Lemma: If t --> t' and t ∈ T_T then t' ∈ T_T. (closure)
Lemma: If v ∈ S_T then v ∈ T_T.
Lemma: If t ∈ T_T and t -->* v with v value then v ∈ S_T. (def)
Lemma (application): If t1 ∈ T_{A->B}, t2 ∈ T_A then t1 t2 ∈ T_B.

For the application lemma, the SN part needs an induction. In TAPL, they prove it by induction on the "reduction size" of t1 and t2? Actually the standard definition of SN used by Tait is: SN(t) := every reduction sequence from t is finite. Then one proves "t1 ∈ T, t2 ∈ T → t1 t2 ∈ T" by induction on (t1's reduction tree, t2's reduction tree) using that any reduct of the app has smaller measure. The value clause for app: if t1 t2 -->* v' with v' value, then the whole reduction can be decomposed? Hmm.

Alternative approach: Define T_T directly as the set of terms that are SN and whose *one-step reducts* are in T_T, plus value condition? That's circular.

Let's set up a double induction: We can use the well-founded induction on the "SN derivation" of t1 and t2. Simpler: use the induction principle of strongly_normalizing twice? We can phrase a lemma:

Lemma SN_app : forall A B t1 t2,
  T (A -> B) t1 -> T A t2 -> SN (tm_app t1 t2).

Proof: We can do induction on H1 : strongly_normalizing t1? Then in case ST_App2 (t1 value), use induction on H2 : strongly_normalizing t2. The goal inside is SN(v t2); to use the IH on t2 we need it available. Since H2 is not in scope of induction on H1? We can do `induction H1` geneeneralizing B t2? Hmm, the nested induction can be done: revert dependent t2; induction H1; intros; induction H2... But the case ST_App1 needs the outer IH applied with t2 and T(t1'). The inner IH on t2 is for the same t1 and smaller t2, but in ST_App1 case t2 is unchanged and t1 decreased; we want outer IH. If we do inner induction first, ST_App1 case would need IH on t1 (not available since SN t1 fixed). So we need mutual/nested induction: outer on SN t1, inner on SN t2. In the ST_App2 case, t1 stays the same but is a value with the same SN t1 derivation possibly; we use inner IH on t2. That works: outer IH: forall t2, SN t2 -> T... -> SN(app t1' t2) for t1' with t1 --> t1'. Inner IH: for the current t1 (value), forall t2', t2 --> t2' -> ... -> SN(app t1 t2'). The T/S style avoids this by having S_arrow only require arguments in S (values), so in the abs case, the app closure doesn't recurse arbitrarily; instead, the value clause handles reducts. Let's stick with the T/S style but figure out the cleanest formalization.

Let me look at how this is typically done in Coq for CBV STLC SN. Since I can't search the web, I'll reason it out.

T/S definition (mutual on ty):
S T v :=
  match T with
  | Bool => v = true \/ v = false
  | Arrow T1 T2 => value v ∧ (forall body, v = tm_abs T1 body -> forall u, S T1 u -> T T2 (open body u))
  end
T T t :=
  SN t ∧ forall t', t -->* t' -> value t' -> S T t'.

Note: S T v implies value v for both branches (Bool: true/false are values; Arrow: explicitly). Good.

Properties:
1. S_T(v) -> value v.
2. value v -> (T_T(v) <-> S_T(v)). Proof: T_T(v) = SN v ∧ all value reducts in S. SN v: values are SN (prove value_irreducible: v --> v' impossible; then SN by SN_intro vacuous). Value reducts: v -->* v' and value v' → v' = v (since no steps) → need S_T(v). Conversely S_T(v) -> T_T(v): SN v ✓; v -->* v' value → v' = v → S_T(v) ✓.
3. T_T(t) -> SN t.
4. T_T(t) -> t --> t' -> T_T(t'): SN t' from SN t; value clause: t' -->* v value → t -->* v via multi_step → S v. ✓.

Wait, SN t' from SN t: given SN t = SN_intro f, f t' H : SN t'. ✓ Simple.

5. Fvars: T_T(fvar x)? SN fvar ✓ (no steps). Value clause: fvar -->* v value → since fvar has no steps, v = fvar; but fvar not a value (value only abs/true/false). So vacuous. Need lemma fvar_step: fvar x --> t' → False. ✓ by inversion. And value_fvar: ~ value (fvar x) by inversion. ✓.

6. Stuck terms: define stuck t := (forall t', ~ t --> t') ∧ ~ value t. Lemma stuck_T: stuck t -> T_T(t) for all T. Proof: SN (no steps). Value clause: t -->* v value. If t has no steps, t -->* v implies v = t (multi only via refl since first step impossible); but ~value t. Prove lemma multi_no_step: (forall t', ~ step t t') -> t -->* v -> v = t. By induction on multi: refl ✓; step case: step t y contradicts. Then value clause vacuous. So stuck_T holds for all T, no induction needed. 

7. App lemma: T_{A->B}(t1) -> T_A(t2) -> T_B(t1 t2).

Proof plan: 
- SN(app): Well-founded induction on SN(t1) and SN(t2). Let's define the statement and prove by induction on SN t1, then inner induction on SN t2.

Actually, maybe there's a slicker way: prove SN(app) using the value-clause of t1 and t2 plus the fact that any reduction sequence from app can be reordered? Hmm, CBV reductions are deterministic up to evaluation order; the app either reduces t1 until value, then t2 until value, then beta. For SN, we need every path finite. We know SN t1: every path t1 --> ... finite; when t1 reaches a value v1, then S_{A->B}(v1) (from T value clause) gives for every u ∈ S_A, T_B(v1 u) hence SN(v1 u). Since SN t2 ensures every path from t2 ends... but does every path from app factor as: a sequence of App1/App2 steps corresponding to reductions of t1 (until value) then t2 (until value) then beta? Yes: reductions of app t1 t2 are determined by reductions of t1 (App1) while t1 not value; once t1 is a value, reductions are App2 of t2 while t2 not value; once both values, ST_AppAbs. But note: ST_App2 requires value t1; before t1 is a value, only App1 steps possible. And ST_App1 requires locally_closed t2 but that's a side condition; if t2 not lc, App1 steps still possible. So the reduction tree of app t1 t2 is: interleaving? There's no interleaving: as long as t1 not a value, only App1 applies; so all steps are App1 until t1 is a value, or the path gets stuck (if t1 gets stuck non-value). If t1 becomes value v1, then steps are App2 on t2 until t2 is a value or stuck; if t2 becomes value u, then AppAbs gives open body u.

SN(app) can be proven by induction on the "lexicographic" SN derivations. Let me formalize in Coq:

Lemma SN_app_aux: forall A B (t1 t2 : tm),
  T (A->B) t1 -> T A t2 -> SN (tm_app t1 t2).

Proof:

Yes! Clean structure:
- Lemma 7a (SN_app_value): forall A B v t2, value v -> T_{A->B}(v) -> T_A(t2) -> SN(tm_app v t2).
  Proof: induction on SN t2 (destruct T_A(t2) to get SN t2 and value clause). For SN(v t2), show all one-step reducts SN:

  Lemma SN_app : forall A B t1 t2, T (A->B) t1 -> T A t2 -> SN (app t1 t2).
  Proof.
    intros A B t1 t2 H1.

  Hmm, the induction on SN1 after destructing T(t1) loses the conjunction; but we can prove a closure lemma: T T t -> t --> t' -> T T t'. It takes T T t as hypothesis.   Idea: Prove T_B(app) as a whole by outer induction on SN t1, with the motive P t1 := forall t2, T A t2 -> T (A->B) t1 -> T B (app t1 t2). Then T_B includes SN and value clause. The induction hypothesis IH1 gives P t1' for reducts. In the SN_intro step, to prove T_B(app t1 t2) = SN(app) ∧ value-clause(app):

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

I need to confirm how Coq generates IH for premises that are themselves foralls. The induction principle for an inductive with constructor
  | T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)
is:
  T_Abs : forall L Gamma T1 T2 t1,
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type ...) ->
      (forall x, ~ In x L -> P (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2) ->
      P Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (T1 -> T2).
Yes, because the recursive occurrence is under the binders x and the implication.

So in the proof, `induction H` with motive P gives in T_Abs case: IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall env S, env_ok (update Γ x T1) env S -> T T2 (subst env (open t1 (fvar x))). We instantiate x := our fresh atom, and pass ~ In x L. ✓.

But there's a catch: Coq's induction principle might not generalize the env and S if they're in the goal but not in the context of H appropriately. We should state the lemma as:

Lemma fundamental : forall Γ t T, has_type Γ t T -> forall env S, env_ok Γ env S -> T T (subst env t).

and prove by `intros Γ t T H. induction H; intros env S Hok.` This will give IH exactly as above (with env S generalized since they're introduced after H). Good.

Now, do we need `S` to be defined via mutual Fixpoint with T? Let's write:

Fixpoint S (T : ty) (v : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => v = tm_true \/ v = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      value v /\
      exists body, v = tm_abs T1 body /\ forall u, S T1 u -> T T2 (open body u)
  end
with T (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' -> S T t'.

Mutual fixpoint on T (ty). Recursive calls: S Bool: none. S Arrow: S T1 (subterm), T T2 (subterm). T: S T (same T!) — this is the problematic non-decreasing mutual call. Coq's guard checker might reject T calling S with the same argument. Hmm. In a mutual fixpoint, the guard condition: Coq requires that recursive calls are on structurally smaller arguments, and for mutual recursion, there's a notion of "principal argument" per function; cross-calls must decrease the argument for the callee? I recall Coq allows mutual fixpoints where the recursion decreases in a well-founded way but syntactically it requires each call to be on a subterm of the caller's corresponding argument. Let's test a tiny example:

Fixpoint even (n:nat) : Prop :=
  match n with 0 => True | S k => odd k end
with odd (n:nat) : Prop :=
  match n with 0 => False | S k => even k end.

This is accepted by Coq. Here even calls odd on k (subterm) and odd calls even on k (subterm). Now what about:

Fixpoint f (n:nat) : Prop := match n with 0 => True | S k => g n end
with g (n:nat) : Prop := match n with 0 => False | S k => f k end.

Here f calls g with n (same, not smaller). Is this accepted? I believe the guard checker tracks for each function its decreasing argument and requires recursive calls to be on structurally smaller terms; for the mutual block to be well-founded, the call graph must decrease... Since f (S k) calls g (S k) which calls f k, this is terminating but the direct call f→g doesn't decrease. Coq's guard condition for mutual fixpoints: it uses a "recursive argument" analysis: In a mutual block, each function has a designated decreasing argument; a call f args from g must have args at f's decreasing position smaller than g's decreasing argument (at the same position) OR... I think Coq requires strict decrease at each call relative to the caller's decreasing argument. So f (S k) → g (S k) would be rejected because n is not a subterm.

Let me just avoid the issue by defining T and S differently: make S a Fixpoint that uses R defined... circular. Alternatively, define T as a Fixpoint on T with S defined inside as a local match, inlining S's definition into T's arrow case. Let's see if we can express everything with just one relation T:

T T t := SN t ∧ forall t', multi t t' -> value t' -> S T t'

where S appears. If we inline S into T, then T's arrow branch contains S T1 u = (match T1 with Bool => ... | Arrow ... => value u ∧ exists body ... T T2' ...). So T's body is:

Fixpoint T (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t ∧
  forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
    match T with
    | Ty_Bool => t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false
    | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
        value t' ∧ exists body, t' = tm_abs T1 body /\ forall u, S T1 u -> T T2 (open body u)
    end

where S T1 u is again a match on T1 with recursive T calls at subterms of T1... but T1 is a subterm of T (in the match branch), and T1's subterms are subterms of T. However, the occurrence is: T is the fixpoint parameter; we match on T, get T1, then match on T1 and call T T2' etc. Coq guard checker: the calls are on subterms of T1 which is a pattern variable of the match on T. Does it recognize nested matches to show subterm? It might, since T1 is a local variable bound by the match that's a subterm of T; then matching T1 and taking its subterms — these are subterms of T by transitivity? Coq's guard checker works with the "match" construct: when you match on T, in the branch, T1 is recognized as a subterm of T. Then matching T1, its subterms are subterms of T1, but does the guard checker know they're subterms of T? It may not transitively track; typically the "subterm" relation is only through match on a variable. There's a known technique: use a nested fixpoint on T1 inside the branch? Not allowed (fixpoint inside fixpoint can be okay if applied to subterms?).

Fixpoint T (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  SN t ∧
  forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => t' = tm_true ∨ t' = tm_false

Hmm. Since S T v and T T t at the same type, and S's clause for arrow refers to T T2 (subterm) and S T1 (subterm), while T's clause refers to S T (same). But note T T t's reference to S T is applied to *values t'*. Could we define, for each type, the value relation and term relation in a single combined predicate? Define:

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (x : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      (SN x ∧ forall t', multi step x t' -> value t' -> (t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      SN x ∧
      (forall t', multi step x t' -> value t' ->
         exists body, t' = tm_abs T1 body /\
         forall u, Rv T1 u -> R T2 (open body u))
      where Rv T1 u := value u ∧ ... 
  end

The issue is again expressing Rv (the value relation) for T1 without a separate Fixpoint. But maybe we can define Rv T1 u := R T1 u ∧ value u! Since we proved (and will prove) that for values v, R T v ↔ S T v, we could use R T1 u ∧ value u in place of S T1 u. Then everything is R only, a single Fixpoint on T:

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      SN t ∧ (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' -> (t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false))
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      SN t ∧
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         forall body, t' = tm_abs T1 body ->
         forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u))
  end.

Recursion: R T1 and R T2 are strict subterms of T = Ty_Arrow T1 T2, obtained by matching on T. This is the standard structurally recursive definition; Coq should accept (calls in the match branches on T1/T2 which are pattern variables). I believe Coq's guard checker accepts `Fixpoint R (T:ty) (t:tm) {struct T} : Prop := match T with Ty_Arrow T1 T2 => ... R T1 ... R T2 ... end` — yes, this is a very common pattern (logical relations in Coq), definitely accepted.

So define R with argument value u and R T1 u in the arrow clause. This replaces S T1 u with "value u ∧ R T1 u". Is that semantically equivalent to our S? Let's define S implicitly as Sval T v := value v ∧ R T v. Then:
- For Bool: Sval_Bool(v) = value v ∧ R_Bool(v) = value v ∧ SN v ∧ all value reducts true/false. Values v: true or false (or abs, but then R_Bool(abs)? R_Bool(abs) = SN abs ∧ all value reducts of abs are true/false: abs -->* abs value → need abs = true ∨ false, false. So R_Bool(abs) is False ✓). For v = true: R_Bool(true) = SN true ∧ (true -->* t' value → t' = true → true=true∨...) ✓. So Sval_Bool(true/false) ✓ and no others. So equivalent-ish to before.
- For arrow: Sval_{A->B}(v) = value v ∧ R_{A->B}(v) = value v ∧ SN v ∧ forall value reducts t' of v: forall body, t'=abs A body → forall u, value u → R A u → R B (open body u). Since v is a value and values are irreducible, the value reducts are v itself; v = abs A body (since value v and type arrow; if v is true/false, no body satisfies... Actually v could be true: value true, but then the condition "forall body, true = abs A body -> ..." is vacuous, so R_{A->B}(true) = value true ∧ SN true ∧ vacuous = True?! Hmm! That means R_{A->B}(true) holds, which is semantically wrong but maybe harmless? Let's check: R_{A->B}(true) = SN true ∧ forall t' multi true t', value t' -> forall body, t' = abs A body -> ... Since true is a value with no steps, t' = true, and true = abs A body is false for any body (no confusion) → the body condition is vacuous. So R_{A->B}(true) = True. That's bad for the app lemma: if t1 = true has R_{A->B}, then app true t2... but t2 has type A; true applied: stuck. T_B(app true t2)? If we're trying to prove R_B(app t1 t2) from R_arrow(t1) and R_A(t2), we use the arrow clause which for t1=true is vacuous, so no info. But is R_B(app true t2) provable otherwise? app true t2: steps: ST_App1 if true steps (no); ST_App2 needs value true and t2 steps; ST_AppAbs needs value true = abs (no). So app true t2 steps only if t2 steps: app true t2 --> app true t2'. So SN depends on SN t2 ✓ (from R_A t2). Value clause: any reduct chain app true t2 -->* s value. Since app true t2 is not a value, the first step must be ST_App2 (t2 --> t2'), and then continue. We can prove R_B(app true t2) by induction on SN t2 similarly to the value lemma: for value v with R_{A->B}(v): 
   - if v = abs A body: AppAbs possible etc.
   - if v = true/false: only App2 steps; induction on SN t2.
  So the value lemma can be generalized to any value v (including true/false) with R_{A->B}(v). Then in the app lemma's ST_App2/AppAbs cases, fine.

But there's a subtle issue: in the T_Abs fundamental case, we prove R_{T1->T2}(abs T1 body). Its value clause: for value reducts t' = abs T1 body, forall body' (t' = abs T1 body'), forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body' u). But env_ok(b) requires R U (env y) for context variables; for the extended x, we need R T1 u ✓ (given), and lc u from value u ✓. For the freshness/substitution equation, env x = fvar x ✓. Good.

But wait, there's a critical difference: previously S_arrow required the body condition only for arguments in S; now R_arrow's value clause requires `value u ∧ R T1 u`. And in the value lemma (for t1 value), the AppAbs case uses the same. For t1 = true (value but not abs), the AppAbs rule doesn't apply; the steps are only App2. Good.

So the single relation R is:

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' -> t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         forall body, t' = tm_abs T1 body ->
         forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u))
  end.

Wait, the arrow clause's `t'` is a value and equals abs T1 body; we could simplify by not quantifying body but pattern: forall t', multi t t' -> value t' -> forall body, t' = tm_abs T1 body -> ... Since t1 : A->B, and t1 = tm_abs A body from inversion, the types align. But when we have value t' and t' = tm_abs T1 body, we need body extraction. Alternative: use a function to extract: forall t', multi t t' -> t' = tm_abs T1 body... The quantification over body with equality is fine.

Hmm, but actually there's a subtlety: R_arrow's value clause for t' = abs T1 body: it says forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u). Good.

Proof. induction l as [| a l [x Hx]].
- exists 0. intros H; inversion H.
- (* need y not in a::l *) 
  destruct (Nat.eq_dec a (S x)) as [E|E].

Let me now define everything concretely.

Definitions:

Definition subst (env : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => env x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst env t1) (subst env t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst env t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst env t1) (subst env t2) (subst env t3)
  end.

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         forall T1' body, t' = tm_abs T1' body ->
         T1' = T1 /\
         forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u))
  end.

Hmm, wait: the type-match condition `T1' = T1` — is it really necessary? Let's reconsider. In R_app_value's AppAbs case, we have v = abs T body from inversion of the step, and HRv's value clause gives for t'=v, body'=?: we need v = tm_abs T1 body' with T1 the relation's domain. If the stored type T ≠ T1, no body' exists and the clause is vacuous. With the type-match condition, the clause gives T1' = T1 for all representations, so given v = abs T body we get T = T1 ✓. Without it, we're stuck. So yes, include type-match.

But wait: is the type-match provable in the fundamental T_Abs? t = tm_abs T1 t1 typed by T_Abs with domain T1; subst env → tm_abs T1 (subst env t1); value reducts: only itself; forall T1' body, tm_abs T1 (subst env t1) = tm_abs T1' body -> T1' = T1 ∧ ...: inversion gives T1' = T1 ✓.

But hmm, what about the body equality: body = subst env t1; the body condition requires forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2(open body u) ✓ via IH.

Now R_app_value: let me write the full proof. Statement:

Lemma R_app_value : forall T1 T2 (v t2 : tm),
  value v -> R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app v t2).

Proof:
  intros T1 T2 v t2 Hv HRv HR2.
  destruct HR2 as [HSN2 HVC2].
  revert T1 T2 v Hv HRv.
  induction HSN2 as [t2 Hstep IH].
  intros T1 T2 v Hv HRv.
  (* need R T2 (app v t2) *)
  destruct T2 as [| T2a T2b].
  - (* T2 = Bool *)
    split.
    + (* SN (app v t2) *)
      apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [Tt tt vv Hlc Hvalv HstepAbs | t1' t2' Ht1' Hlc2 | v1 t2' Hval1 Ht2' Hstep2 | ... | ... | ...].
      Actually inversion on step with conclusion tm_app v t2 --> s gives cases ST_AppAbs, ST_App1, ST_App2.
      * ST_AppAbs: premises: locally_closed (tm_abs T t), value vv, and v = tm_abs T t, t2 = vv, s = open t vv. Need SN (open t vv). We have HRv : R (T1->Bool) v. Its value clause with t' = v (multi_refl), value v, T1' = T, body = t, and v = tm_abs T t gives T = T1 and forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R Bool (open t u). Apply with u := vv: value vv (from Hvalv) and R T1 vv. Do we have R T1 vv? We have HVC? Wait, we have HR2 destructed as HSN2 HVC2 and we're in the induction; the IH is about smaller t2. In the ST_AppAbs case, the argument is vv = t2, and we need R T1 t2. But we destructed HR2 into HSN2 and HVC2, losing HR2 itself. Hmm, we need R T1 t2 in this case; we can reconstruct: R T1 t2 = conj (SN_intro Hstep IH) HVC2. Since we did induction on HSN2, the original SN derivation is SN_intro Hstep IH. Now R_app (general application lemma):

Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 (t1 t2 : tm),

Now the fundamental theorem and its T_Abs case:

Lemma fundamental : forall Γ t T, has_type Γ t T -> forall env S, env_ok Γ env S -> R T (subst env t).

T_Abs case:
  H : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Γ x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2.
  IH : forall x, ~ In x L -> forall env S, env_ok (update Γ x T1) env S -> R T2 (subst env (open t1 (fvar x))).
  Goal: R (T1->T2) (subst env (tm_abs T1 t1)) = R (T1->T2)(tm_abs T1 (subst env t1)).
  Let body := subst env t1.
  Need: SN(abs T1 body) ∧ value clause.
  - SN: no steps from abs → SN_intro (intros t' Hstep; inversion Hstep). Lemma SN_abs.
  - value clause: intros t' Hmulti Hval. From Hmulti and abs having no steps: t' = tm_abs T1 body. Then intros T1' body' Heq. Injection Heq gives T1' = T1 and body' = body. Split: T1' = T1 ✓; intros u Hvalu HRu.
    Need R T2(open body u) where body = subst env t1.
    Pick x fresh: x ∉ L, x ∉ fvs t1, x ∉ S. Let env' := extend env x u.
    Have Hty : has_type (update Γ x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2 := H x HxL.
    Have Hok' : env_ok (update Γ x T1) env' (x :: S).
    Then IH x HxL env' (x::S) Hok' : R T2 (subst env' (open t1 (fvar x))).
    Rewrite subst_open_fvar: subst env' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst env t1) u. Note open t1 (fvar x) = open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1 ✓.
    Need env x = fvar x: from Hok(c) with x ∉ S ✓.
    Need lc(env y) for all y: Hok(a) ✓.
    Need x ∉ fvs t1 ✓.
    Then the equation gives the goal ✓.

Now let's nail down the substitution equation lemma.

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall t x u env k,
  ~ In x (fvs t) ->
  env x = tm_fvar x ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (env y)) ->
  subst (extend env x u) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k u (subst env t).

Proof by induction on t; generalize k.
- tm_bvar i: destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
  + (* i = k *) open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = fvar x; subst (extend env x u) (fvar x) = extend env x u x = u (since Nat.eqb x x = true). RHS open_rec k u (subst env (bvar i)) = open_rec k u (bvar i) = if k =? i then u else bvar i = u ✓.
  + LHS: subst (open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i)) = subst (bvar i) = bvar i. RHS: open_rec k u (bvar i) = bvar i since i ≠ k ✓.
- tm_fvar y: Need ~ In x (fvs (fvar y)) means x ≠ y. Wait, the hypothesis ~ In x (fvs t) with fvs (fvar y) = [y].
  LHS: open_rec k (fvar x) (fvar y) = fvar y; subst (extend env x u) (fvar y) = extend env x u y = env y (since y ≠ x; need Nat.eqb x y = false). 
  RHS: subst env (fvar y) = env y; open_rec k u (env y) = env y by lc_open_rec_id (lc(env y)) ✓.
- tm_app t1 t2: IH for t1, t2 with same k; need ~ In x (fvs t1) and ~ In x (fvs t2) from ~ In x (fvs t1 ++ fvs t2) (in_app_or / not_in_app). Then subst/open_rec commute with app ✓.
- tm_abs T t1: LHS: open_rec k (fvar x) (abs T t1) = abs T (open_rec (S k) (fvar x) t1); subst (extend ...) (abs ...) = abs T (subst (extend ...) (open_rec (S k) ... t1)). RHS: subst env (abs T t1) = abs T (subst env t1); open_rec k u (abs T (subst env t1)) = abs T (open_rec (S k) u (subst env t1)). Apply IH with k := S k and t1; need ~ In x (fvs t1) = fvs (abs T t1) ✓ (fvs abs = fvs body).
- true/false: both sides same.
- if: IH on three subterms with the app-style fvs concatenation.

Need lemmas:
- fvs_not_in_app : ~ In x (fvs (app a b)) -> ~ In x (fvs a) ∧ ~ In x (fvs b). Using in_app_iff: In x (l1 ++ l2) ↔ In x l1 ∨ In x l2. `apply in_app_iff`. Simp with fvs. We can prove by intros H [H1|H2]; apply H; apply in_app_iff; auto.
  Might need List.in_app_iff from Stdlib.
- extend_same: extend env x u x = u. Unfold extend; rewrite Nat.eqb_refl; reflexivity.
- extend_other: extend env x u y = env y when x ≠ y. Unfold; rewrite Nat.eqb_neq? Need (x =? y) = false when x ≠ y: apply Nat.eqb_neq; auto. `Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y, (x =? y) = false <-> x <> y`.
- lc_open_rec_id: lc_at k t -> forall j u, k <= j -> open_rec j u t = t.

  - bvar i: lc_at k (bvar i) → i < k ≤ j → i ≠ j. destruct (Nat.eqb j i) eqn:E; [apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; lia | reflexivity].
  - fvar: reflexivity.
  - app/abs/if: simpl; rewrite IHs; under abs, IH with k := S k, j := S j; need S k <= S j from k ≤ j via lia.
  Also lc_at monotonicity: lc_at k t -> k ≤ m -> lc_at m t. Prove by induction on t generalizing k,m? Or induction on the lc_at derivation. Induction on t is easy:
    - bvar: i < k ≤ m ✓.
    - abs: lc_at (S k) body → IH (S k ≤ S m) → lc_at (S m) body → lc_at m (abs) ✓.
  Or we can get monotonicity from lc_open_rec_id? No, prove directly.

Lemma R_if : forall T (b x y : tm),
  R Ty_Bool b -> R T x -> R T y -> R T (tm_if b x y).

  assert (HSNb := R_sn Ty_Bool b HRb).
  revert T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  induction HSNb as [b Hstep IH].
  intros T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  destruct T as [| A B].
  - (* T = Bool *)
    split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. inversion Hs; subst.
      * ST_IfTrue: b = tm_true, x/y lc, s = x. Apply R_sn Bool x HRx.
      * ST_IfFalse: s = y. R_sn ✓.

The induction motive P b := forall T x y, R T x -> R T y -> R Bool b -> R T(if b x y). IH : forall b', b --> b' -> P b'. So apply IH b' Hstep' with T := Bool, x, y, HRx, HRy, and R Bool b' ✓.
      * ST_App-like cases don't apply to if.
    + value clause: intros s Hmulti Hvals. Decompose Hmulti:
      * refl: s = tm_if b x y; value(s) → contradiction (if is not a value) via inversion.
      * step: if b x y --> s1; inversion; cases:

        - ST_IfFalse ✓.
        - ST_If: s1 = if b' x y; R Bool s1 from IH (with R Bool b' via R_step); apply its value clause ✓.

Lemma R_step : forall T t t', R T t -> t --> t' -> R T t'.
Proof.
  intros T; destruct T as [| A B]; intros t t' HR Hstep.
  - destruct HR as [HSN HVC]. split.
    + (* SN t' *) destruct HSN as [Hf]; apply Hf; exact Hstep. 
      Hmm: HSN : strongly_normalizing t. `inversion HSN` or destruct HSN as [Hf]; Hf : forall t', t --> t' -> SN t'. So apply Hf; exact Hstep.
    + intros s Hmulti Hval. apply HVC. apply multi_step with t'; assumption.
  - destruct HR as [HSN HVC]. split.
    + destruct HSN as [Hf]; apply Hf; exact Hstep.
    + intros s Hmulti Hval T' body Heq. apply HVC with (t' := s) (T1' := T') (body := body); auto using multi_step.
Qed.

Lemma R_sn : forall T t, R T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T; destruct T; intros t HR; exact (proj1 HR). Qed.

Prove by induction on multi; step case contradicts. ✓ We have step_abs_none: forall T b s, ~ (tm_abs T b --> s) by inversion on step.
    Then subst Ht'; injection Heq; intros; subst; split; [reflexivity | intros u Hvalu HRu].
    Now the main goal: R T2 (open (subst env t1) u).
    Choose x fresh from L, fvs t1, S. Let env' := extend env x u.
    Have IH x HxL : forall env S, env_ok (update Γ x T1) env S -> R T2 (subst env (open t1 (fvar x))).

      (c) forall y, ~ In y (x::S) -> env' y = tm_fvar y.

    Then the equation: subst env' (open t1 (fvar x)) = open (subst env t1) u.

(* free variables *)
Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => []
  | tm_fvar x => [x]
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => []
  | tm_false => []
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

(* substitution of free variables *)

Now, the multi_step_decompose lemma:
Lemma multi_step_decompose : forall t s, multi step t s -> t = s \/ exists s1, step t s1 /\ multi step s1 s.
Proof. intros t s H; induction H.
 - left; reflexivity.
 - right. exists y. split; assumption.
Qed.

(* ===== Logical relation development ===== *)

Definition extend (env : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else env y.

Fixpoint subst (env : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => env x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst env t1) (subst env t2)
  | tm_abs T1 t1 => tm_abs T1 (subst env t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst env t1) (subst env t2) (subst env t3)
  end.

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => cons x nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => app (fvs t1) (fvs t2)
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => app (app (fvs t1) (fvs t2)) (fvs t3)
  end.
```

Lemma maxlist_ub : forall l x, In x l -> x <= maxlist l.
Proof. induction l as [|a l IH]; intros x H; simpl in *; destruct H as [->|H]; [apply Nat.le_max_l | apply Nat.le_trans with (maxlist l); [apply IH; auto | apply Nat.le_max_r]]. Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.

Now the relation R. Let me define it exactly:

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         t' = tm_true \/ t' = tm_false)
  | Ty_Arrow T1 T2 =>
      strongly_normalizing t /\
      (forall t', multi step t t' -> value t' ->
         forall T1' body, t' = tm_abs T1' body ->
         T1' = T1 /\
         forall u, value u -> R T1 u -> R T2 (open body u))
  end.

Theorem normalization : forall t T, <{ empty |-- t \in T }> -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof.
  intros t T H.
  assert (Hok : env_ok empty (fun x => tm_fvar x) nil).
  { unfold env_ok. repeat split.
    - intros y; simpl; constructor.   (* locally_closed (fvar y) = lc_at 0 (fvar y) *)
    - intros y U Hy; unfold empty in Hy; discriminate. 
    - intros y Hy; reflexivity. }
  specialize (fundamental empty t T H (fun x => tm_fvar x) nil Hok).
  (* R T (subst (fun x => fvar x) t) *)
  rewrite subst_identity in H. apply R_sn in H? 
  Let me define subst_identity : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
  Then `rewrite subst_identity in H` gives H : R T t; apply R_sn in H; exact H.
Qed.

Lemma subst_identity : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof. induction t; simpl; try reflexivity; try (rewrite IHt; reflexivity); ...
 - tm_app: rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity.
 - tm_abs: rewrite IHt; reflexivity.
 - tm_if: rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3; reflexivity.
 - tm_fvar: reflexivity (simpl gives tm_fvar x = tm_fvar x).
Qed.

```coq
From Stdlib Require Import Arith.PeanoNat.
From Stdlib Require Import Lists.List.
From Stdlib Require Import Relations.Relation_Definitions.
From Stdlib Require Import Lia.

Module STLCCBVNormalizationTask.

(* ... all given definitions ... *)

(* ===== begin added ===== *)

Definition extend (env : atom -> tm) (x : atom) (u : tm) : atom -> tm :=
  fun y => if Nat.eqb x y then u else env y.

Fixpoint subst (env : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => env x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst env t1) (subst env t2)
  | tm_abs T1 t1 => tm_abs T1 (subst env t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst env t1) (subst env t2) (subst env t3)
  end.

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

Lemma subst_identity : forall t, subst (fun x => tm_fvar x) t = t.
Proof.
  induction t; simpl; try reflexivity;
    try (rewrite IHt; reflexivity);
    try (rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity);
    try (rewrite IHt1, IHt2, IHt3; reflexivity).
Qed.
```

Hmm, `try (rewrite IHt; reflexivity)` etc. may leave goals; better write each case. Actually, for tm_bvar: simpl gives tm_bvar n = tm_bvar n? subst env (tm_bvar i) = tm_bvar i ✓ reflexivity. tm_fvar: env x = fvar x ✓. tm_app: rewrite IHt1, IHt2 ✓. tm_abs: rewrite IHt ✓. tm_true/false ✓. tm_if: rewrite IHt1..3 ✓.

With the try-chain: in tm_app, `try (rewrite IHt; reflexivity)` doesn't apply (IHt doesn't exist; there are IHt1, IHt2) — `rewrite IHt` would fail, caught. But then `try (rewrite IHt1, IHt2; reflexivity)` applies. In tm_abs, IHt exists. Hmm, the induction names depend on the constructor: for tm_abs T t1, the IH is IHt1. Let me just write explicit cases with `induction t as [...]`. Simpler to write:

Proof.
  intros t. induction t as [i | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T1 t1 IH1 | | | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3]; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2; reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1; reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - reflexivity.
  - rewrite IH1, IH2, IH3; reflexivity.
Qed.

Note: `induction t as` patterns for tm: tm_bvar (nat) → i; tm_fvar (atom) → x; tm_app t1 t2 → t1 IH1 t2 IH2 (order: arguments then IHs); tm_abs ty tm → T1 t1 IH1; tm_true; tm_false; tm_if t1 t2 t3 → t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3. Good.

Next, maxlist and fresh.

```coq
Fixpoint maxlist (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | cons a l' => Nat.max a (maxlist l')
  end.

Lemma maxlist_ub : forall l x, In x l -> x <= maxlist l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x H; simpl in *.
  - destruct H.
  - destruct H as [-> | H].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (maxlist l); [apply IH; auto | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_atom : forall l : list atom, exists x, ~ In x l.
Proof.
  intros l. exists (S (maxlist l)). intro H.
  pose proof (maxlist_ub l _ H). lia.
Qed.

Lemma fresh3 : forall l1 l2 l3 : list atom,
  exists x, ~ In x l1 /\ ~ In x l2 /\ ~ In x l3.
Proof.
  intros l1 l2 l3.
  destruct (fresh_atom (l1 ++ l2 ++ l3)) as [x Hx].
  exists x. repeat split; intros H.
  - apply Hx. apply in_app_iff. left. exact H.
  - apply Hx. apply in_app_iff. right. apply in_app_iff. left. exact H.
  - apply Hx. apply in_app_iff. right. apply in_app_iff. right. exact H.
Qed.
```

Note: l1 ++ l2 ++ l3 with right associativity = l1 ++ (l2 ++ l3) ✓. But is `++` right associative in Coq's List? Yes: `Infix "++" := app (right associativity, at level 60) : list_scope.` So the above works.

Fixpoint R (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=

Lemma subst_open_rec : forall t x u env k,
  ~ In x (fvs t) ->
  env x = tm_fvar x ->
  (forall y, locally_closed (env y)) ->
  subst (extend env x u) (open_rec k (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec k u (subst env t).

Proof by induction on t (generalizing k):
intros t. induction t as [...]; intros x u env k Hfresh Henvx Hlc; simpl.
- bvar i: simpl. destruct (Nat.eqb k i) eqn:E.
  + open_rec k (fvar x) (bvar i) = fvar x; subst (extend ...) (fvar x) = extend env x u x = u. RHS: open_rec k u (subst env (bvar i)) = open_rec k u (bvar i) = if k =? i then u else bvar i = u ✓.
    After simpl, the goal might be: (if x =? x then u else env x) = (if k =? i then u else bvar i). With E : (k =? i) = true, RHS = u; LHS: if x =? x then u else env x = u by Nat.eqb_refl.
  + RHS = bvar i; LHS: open_rec k (fvar x)(bvar i) = bvar i (since E false), then subst ... (bvar i) = bvar i ✓.
- fvar y: Hfresh : ~ In x (fvs (tm_fvar y)) = ~ In x (y :: nil), so x <> y.
  After simpl: subst (extend env x u) (open_rec k (fvar x) (fvar y)) = open_rec k u (subst env (fvar y)).
  open_rec k (fvar x) (fvar y) = fvar y; subst ... (fvar y) = extend env x u y. RHS: subst env (fvar y) = env y; open_rec k u (env y).
  Goal: extend env x u y = open_rec k u (env y).
  extend env x u y = if x =? y then u else env y = env y since x ≠ y (need (x =? y) = false).
  open_rec k u (env y) = env y by lc_open_rec_id with lc_at 0 (env y) and 0 <= k.
  Hfresh gives x <> y: intro; apply Hfresh; left; exact H? Hfresh : ~ In x [y]; if x = y then In x [y]. Use: assert (Hxy : x <> y) by (intro E; apply Hfresh; left; exact E). Then rewrite Nat.eqb_neq? `apply Nat.eqb_neq in Hxy`? Nat.eqb_neq : forall x y, (x =? y) = false <-> x <> y. So `rewrite (proj2 (Nat.eqb_neq x y) Hxy)` or `apply Nat.eqb_neq; exact Hxy`? Hmm, Nat.eqb_neq is an iff; if goal is `(x =? y) = false`, apply Nat.eqb_neq? `apply Nat.eqb_neq` might work if it's stated as `(x =? y) = false <-> x <> y`, then apply expects to prove the RHS? Actually `apply Nat.eqb_neq` with goal `(x =? y) = false` will convert to proving `x <> y` if the lemma's conclusion matches the goal (the forward direction). Let's just use `rewrite Nat.eqb_neq`? We'll experiment.
  Alternatively `destruct (Nat.eqb x y) eqn:E; [apply Nat.eqb_eq in E; subst; exfalso; apply Hfresh; left; reflexivity | reflexivity]`.
- app t1 t2: Hfresh : ~ In x (fvs t1 ++ fvs t2). Derive Hf1 : ~ In x (fvs t1), Hf2 : ~ In x (fvs t2).
  simpl open_rec: app (open_rec k (fvar x) t1)(open_rec k (fvar x) t2). subst: app (subst ...)(subst ...). RHS app (open_rec k u (subst env t1))(open_rec k u (subst env t2)). Rewrite IH1 with (k := k), IH2. 
- abs T1 t1: Hfresh : ~ In x (fvs (tm_abs T1 t1)) = ~ In x (fvs t1).
  simpl: open_rec k (fvar x)(tm_abs T1 t1) = tm_abs T1 (open_rec (S k)(fvar x) t1); subst (extend ...) (tm_abs ...) = tm_abs T1 (subst (extend...) (open_rec (S k) ... t1)); RHS: tm_abs T1 (subst env t1); open_rec k u (tm_abs T1 (subst env t1)) = tm_abs T1 (open_rec (S k) u (subst env t1)). Apply IH1 with (k := S k).
- true/false: reflexivity.
- if: fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3; derive three; rewrite IH1..3.

Now, ~ In x (a ++ b) derivation: In x (a++b) ↔ In x a ∨ In x b via in_app_iff. Write a helper:

Lemma not_in_app_inv : forall x l1 l2, ~ In x (l1 ++ l2) -> ~ In x l1 /\ ~ In x l2.
Proof. intros x l1 l2 H; split; intros Hin; apply H; apply in_app_iff; auto. Qed.

Then for if's fvs t1 ++ (fvs t2 ++ fvs t3): first split gives ~In t1 and ~In (fvs t2 ++ fvs t3); split again.

Actually careful: fvs (tm_if t1 t2 t3) = fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3 = fvs t1 ++ (fvs t2 ++ fvs t3) (right assoc). ✓.

Now the final lemma:
Lemma subst_open : forall t x u env,
  ~ In x (fvs t) -> env x = tm_fvar x -> (forall y, locally_closed (env y)) ->
  subst (extend env x u) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst env t) u.
Proof. intros; unfold open. apply subst_open_rec; auto. Qed.

Now R_app_value. Let me write it carefully. I'll need helper lemmas for step inversion perhaps.

Let me write the proof and iterate:

Lemma R_app_value : forall T1 T2 (v t2 : tm),
  value v -> R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) v -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app v t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 v t2 Hv HRv HR2.
  assert (HSN2 : strongly_normalizing t2) by (apply (R_sn T1 t2 HR2)).
  revert T2 v Hv HRv HR2.
  induction HSN2 as [t2 Hstep2 IH].
  intros T2 v Hv HRv HR2.
  destruct T2 as [| C D].
  - (* T2 = Bool *)
    destruct HRv as [HSNv HVCv].
    split.
    + (* SN (tm_app v t2) *)
      apply SN_intro. intros s Hs. inversion Hs as [T body vv Hlc Hval | t1' t2' Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2' Hval1 Hstep2' Hstep22]; subst.
      * (* ST_AppAbs: v = tm_abs T body, t2 = vv, s = open body vv *)
        ...
      * (* ST_App1 *)
      * (* ST_App2 *)
    + (* value clause *)
      ...
  - (* T2 = Arrow C D *) ...
Qed.

Hmm, `inversion Hs as [...]` patterns: the step constructors are:
ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, locally_closed (tm_abs T t) -> value v -> tm_app (tm_abs T t) v --> open t v
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> locally_closed t2 -> tm_app t1 t2 --> tm_app t1' t2
ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> tm_app v1 t2 --> tm_app v1 t2'
ST_IfTrue ...
etc.

When inverting Hs : step (tm_app v t2) s, only the app constructors can unify (ST_IfTrue etc. have conclusions tm_if ...). Inversion should yield 3 cases. With explicit pattern `as [T body vv Hlc Hval | t1' t2' Hstep1 Hlc2 | v1 t2' Hval1 Hstep2' Hstep22]`: the third constructor ST_App2's args: v1 t2 t2' Hval1 Hstep2 — that's 5 args: v1, t2 (the term), t2' (the reduct), value v1, step t2 t2'. Pattern: [v1 t2' Hval1 Hstep2' ...] hmm, the pattern must list all args: v1, t2, t2', Hval, Hstep. But `t2` in the constructor will unify with our t2 and get substituted; the intro pattern name for it... In inversion patterns, the arguments that are determined by unification may be filled automatically. It's safer to not use explicit patterns and instead use `inversion Hs; subst; clear Hs` and let Coq name things, then use `match goal with`? That's fragile.

Alternative: prove dedicated inversion lemmas with explicit existential statements. That might actually make the proofs much more robust:

Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 s,
  tm_app t1 t2 --> s ->
  (exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ t2 = v /\ locally_closed (tm_abs T body) /\ value v /\ s = open body v)
  \/ (exists t1', t1 --> t1' /\ locally_closed t2 /\ s = tm_app t1' t2)
  \/ (exists v1 t2', t1 = v1 /\ value v1 /\ t2 --> t2' /\ s = tm_app v1 t2').
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 s H. inversion H; subst; eauto 10.
Qed.

Then in proofs: destruct (step_app_inv _ _ _ Hs) as [[T [body [vv [Heq1 [Heq2 [Hlc [Hval HeqS]]]]]]] | [[t1' [Hstep1 [Hlc2 HeqS]]] | [v1 [t2' [Heq1 [Hval [Hstep2 HeqS]]]]]]].

This is verbose but predictable. Similarly:

Lemma step_if_inv : forall b x y s,
  tm_if b x y --> s ->
  (b = tm_true /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = x)
  \/ (b = tm_false /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = y)
  \/ (exists b', b --> b' /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = tm_if b' x y).

   inversion H; subst.
   - left. eexists. eexists. eexists. repeat split; eauto.   (* might work *)

But the number of cases after inversion could include the if rules? Inversion on step with conclusion tm_app t1 t2: the if constructors' conclusions are tm_if..., which cannot unify with tm_app... (constructors disjoint), so inversion yields only the 3 app cases. Usually Coq's inversion does discriminate. Let's test.

Similarly for if: inversion on step (tm_if b x y) s yields 3 if cases.

Now R_app. Similar structure:

Lemma R_app : forall T1 T2 (t1 t2 : tm),
  R (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 -> R T1 t2 -> R T2 (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros T1 T2 t1 t2 HR1 HR2.
  assert (HSN1 : strongly_normalizing t1) by (apply (R_sn (Ty_Arrow T1 T2) t1 HR1)).
  revert T2 t2 HR1 HR2.
  induction HSN1 as [t1 Hstep1 IH].
  intros T2 t2 HR1 HR2.
  destruct T2 as [| C D].
  - destruct HR1 as [HSN1' HVC1].
    split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
      destruct (step_app_inv t1 t2 s Hs) as [...].
      * AppAbs: get HVC1 with t' := tm_abs T body (multi_refl? t1 = tm_abs T body after subst) and value (v_abs T body Hlc) → T = T1 ∧ Hbody. Then Hbody vv Hval (R T1 vv). R T1 vv: after t2 = vv, HR2 ✓. R_sn Bool.
      * App1: t1 --> t1', s = app t1' t2. Apply R_sn with T2 := Bool: IH t1' Hstep1 T2 t2 (R_step HR1 Hstep1) HR2.
      * App2: t1 = v1 value, t2 --> t2', s = app v1 t2'. Apply R_sn Bool: R_app_value T1 T2 v1 t2' with value v1, HR1 (after subst t1 = v1), R_step HR2 Hstep2.
    + value clause: intros s Hm Hvl. multi_step_decompose:
      * refl: inversion Hvl.
      * step s1:
        - AppAbs: R Bool(open body vv) from HVC1; its value clause.
        - App1: R Bool(app t1' t2) from IH (with R_step HR1 Hstep1) → its value clause.
        - App2: R Bool(app v1 t2') from R_app_value → its value clause.
  - T2 = Arrow C D: analogous with R (C->D).

The tricky bit is the AppAbs case where t1 = tm_abs T body; after substitution, HVC1's t' argument: use `HVC1 (tm_abs T body) (multi_refl _ _) (v_abs T body Hlc) T body eq_refl`. But HVC1 mentions t1 which may have been substituted to tm_abs T body by `subst`. Since Heq1 : t1 = tm_abs T body, `subst t1` would replace t1 everywhere, including the induction hypotheses Hstep1 and IH — which might be problematic but is usually OK? Substituting the induction variable could break the IH's usability in other branches — but we're already in one branch; after subst, IH : forall t1', tm_abs T body --> t1' -> P t1'. In other branches (App1), we need IH with t1' where t1 --> t1'; but if t1 was substituted only in this branch (we did destruct the step), that's fine — each branch is separate. In the App1 branch, we don't subst t1.

But there's a catch: `subst t1` when t1 appears in Hstep1 : forall t', t1 --> t' -> SN t' (the SN_intro premise) and IH; the subst tactic will try to substitute everywhere, possibly failing or looping if t1 appears in its own definition? t1 = tm_abs T body doesn't contain t1, so subst should work. Alternatively, avoid subst and use equations to rewrite as needed:
  In the AppAbs case with Heq1 : t1 = tm_abs T body, Heq2 : t2 = vv, HeqS : s = open body vv:
  - We need HR1 : R (T1->T2)(tm_abs T body) to apply HVC1; rewrite Heq1 in HR1? But HVC1 was destructed from HR1; HVC1 mentions t1 (the variable). Rewrite Heq1 in HVC1: HVC1 : forall t', multi t1 t' -> ...; `rewrite Heq1 in HVC1` gives HVC1 about tm_abs T body. ✓
  - HR2 : R T1 t2; `rewrite Heq2 in HR2` gives R T1 vv ✓.
  - s: `subst s` or `rewrite HeqS`; goal SN s → rewrite HeqS.
  This avoids substituting t1/t2 globally.

Since HVC1's statement is under binders (forall t'), rewrite in HVC1 should work? `rewrite Heq1 in HVC1` rewriting inside the forall's body — yes, setoid rewriting under binders is allowed for eq.

Hmm, but Heq1 : t1 = tm_abs T body; rewriting with it in HVC1 replaces t1 by tm_abs T body ✓.

Lemma R_if : forall T (b x y : tm),
  R Ty_Bool b -> R T x -> R T y -> R T (tm_if b x y).
Proof.
  intros T b x y HRb HRx HRy.
  assert (HSNb : strongly_normalizing b) by (apply (R_sn Ty_Bool b HRb)).
  revert T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  induction HSNb as [b Hstepb IHb].
  intros T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  destruct T as [| A B].
  - (* T = Bool *)
    destruct HRb as [HSNb' HVCb].
    split.
    + apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
      destruct (step_if_inv b x y s Hs) as
        [[Heqb [Hlcx [Hlcy Heqs]]]
        | [[Heqb [Hlcx [Hlcy Heqs]]]
        | [b' [Hstepb' [Hlcx [Hlcy Heqs]]]]]].
      * rewrite Heqb in HRb? HRb was destructed. We need R Bool x = HRx; subst s; rewrite Heqs; apply R_sn Bool HRx. (HRx : R Ty_Bool x.)
      * apply R_sn Bool HRy.
      * subst s. apply R_sn Bool.
        apply IHb with (b' := b') (H := Hstepb')? IHb : forall b', b --> b' -> P b'. Apply IHb b' Hstepb' with T := Ty_Bool, x, y, HRx, HRy, R_step HRb Hstepb' — wait, HRb was destructed into HSNb' and HVCb. Need R Bool b' : R_step Ty_Bool b b' (conj (SN_intro Hstepb IHb) HVCb) Hstepb'. Reconstruct.
        Hmm, this reconstruction is needed in several places. Maybe don't destruct HRb before the induction? But we need HSNb for the induction; we can get it via R_sn and keep HRb intact! Then:
        assert (HSNb := R_sn Ty_Bool b HRb). revert T x y HRx HRy HRb. induction HSNb. intros. destruct T. ...
        Then HRb : R Ty_Bool b is reverted and reintroduced intact ✓. 
    + value clause: intros s Hm Hvl. multi_step_decompose; step cases:
      * IfTrue: b = tm_true, s1 = x; HRx's value clause: destruct HRx (T = Bool concrete) as [HSNx HVCx]; apply HVCx with s Htail Hvl.
      * IfFalse: HRy's.
      * If: R Bool(if b' x y) from IHb (needs R Bool b' via R_step HRb Hstepb', HRx, HRy); destruct; apply value clause with Htail.
  - T = Arrow A B: analogous, using R_sn (A->B) for the SN part and the value clause of the corresponding R (A->B) hypothesis. Note HRx, HRy : R (A->B) x/y; destruct gives HVCx/HVCy of the arrow shape.

Wait, in the Arrow branch's SN part, the reducts:
  - IfTrue: s = x; apply R_sn (A->B) x HRx.
  - IfFalse: HRy.
  - If: apply R_sn (A->B) (tm_if b' x y) with the R from IHb.
Value clause: goal forall s, multi (if b x y) s -> value s -> forall T1' body, s = tm_abs T1' body -> T1' = A ∧ forall u, ...; use HVCx from destruct HRx : R (A->B) x.

Careful: in R_if's induction, the motive P b := forall T x y, R T x -> R T y -> R Ty_Bool b -> R T (if b x y). Order of arguments in revert: `revert T x y HRx HRy HRb` → goal: forall T x y, R T x -> R T y -> R Ty_Bool b -> R T(if b x y). The motive's last argument order: T x y, then HRx HRy HRb. When applying IHb: IHb b' Hstepb' T x y HRx HRy HRb' where HRb' : R Ty_Bool b'. ✓.

Now the fundamental theorem. Let me write it.

Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T,
  has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall env S, env_ok Gamma env S -> R T (subst env t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as
    [Gamma x T0 Hvar
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hbody IHbody
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 H2 IH1 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 H2 H3 IH1 IH2 IH3];
    intros env S Hok.

For step_if_inv:
Lemma step_if_inv : forall (b x y s : tm),
  tm_if b x y --> s ->
  (b = tm_true /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = x)
  \/ (b = tm_false /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = y)
  \/ (exists b', b --> b' /\ locally_closed x /\ locally_closed y /\ s = tm_if b' x y).
Proof. intros b x y s H. inversion H; subst.
  - left. repeat split; eauto.
  - right; left. repeat split; eauto.
  - right; right. eexists. repeat split; eauto.
Qed.

Lemma R_if : forall T (b x y : tm),
  R Ty_Bool b -> R T x -> R T y -> R T (tm_if b x y).
Proof.
  intros T b x y HRb HRx HRy.
  assert (HSNb : strongly_normalizing b) by (apply (R_sn Ty_Bool b HRb)).
  revert T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  induction HSNb as [b Hstepb IHb].
  intros T x y HRx HRy HRb.
  destruct T as [| A B].
  - (* T = Bool *)
    split.

      destruct (step_if_inv b x y s Hs) as

        |[[Heqb [Hlcx [Hlcy Heqs]]]

      * rewrite Heqs. apply (R_sn Ty_Bool y). exact HRy.

        apply (IHb b' Hstepb' Ty_Bool x y HRx HRy).

    + intros s Hm Hvl.
